Without imports, the first declaration's doc comment documents the module. `cargo xtask oracle docs` records 22 cases with each elm's `docs.json`, and the ratchet `docs_reports` passes all 66 runs.
- [x] **7.4a** `oracle: corpus docs.json`. Every corpus package run through `elm make --docs` for each Compat that accepts it, offline, in an `ELM_HOME` holding the dependencies the development cache has. **Check:** `docs.json` is byte-identical for every corpus package each Compat accepts, and the rejected ones fail with upstream's error.

  Done: `cargo xtask oracle corpus-docs` runs each elm offline on every corpus package with an `ELM_HOME` holding every release the development cache has (656 after `corpus names`) and a registry listing exactly those, now written by `sandbox::registry_of` with several versions a package. It stores a hash of each `docs.json`, or of the report, in `corpus/docs/oracle.tsv`. `cargo xtask corpus docs` solves dependencies as `corpus names` does (now shared as `names::solve`) and compares: all 5,652 package runs elm completes match, `docs.json` byte for byte, including 12 rejected with compile errors and the one whose dependencies have no solution; 51 are skipped for releases the registry no longer serves as published. Surprises: elms sharing an `ELM_HOME` race on a dependency's `artifacts.dat` and die without a report, or very rarely hang; the oracle retries both, and kills a run after ten minutes.
- [x] **7.5** `oracle: whole-project snapshots`. `elm make --output=/dev/null --report=json` on every corpus project, for each Compat, offline, in an `ELM_HOME` like 7.4a's; rejected projects are expected-failure cases. **Check:** `cargo xtask corpus check` compares `nomi check --report=json` with each elm's report, or its silence, on every project.

  Done: `oracle::corpus_runs` now records both corpus oracles: `cargo xtask oracle corpus-check` runs each elm on every app (its `elm.json` naming the Compat) and package, offline, into `corpus/check/oracle.tsv`, where `ok` is silence. The `ELM_HOME`s are shared with `corpus-docs` and kept, with the dependencies elm built, until the cache's releases change, so a full run takes minutes, not hours. `corpus::runs` compares both suites; `cargo xtask corpus check` runs `nomi check --report=json` and finds all 5,664 runs as elm made them: every app and accepted package silent, the 12 runs elm rejects with compile errors (kernel imports where elm won't take them, and one package's today's dependencies) with the same report, and those whose dependencies have no solution as elm found none. 51 are skipped for releases the registry no longer serves as published. Surprise: concurrent elms on one home now and then succeed but write to stderr; alone they are silent, so the oracle retries those too.
- [x] **7.5a** `oracle: CLI report matrix`. Port the old 605-case CLI report matrix (`conformance/checkpoints/2026-09-23/json-reports.py` and `json-report-fixtures`), which compares every output stream, exit status and artifact, to cases recorded from each elm. Cases that need generated JavaScript stay in the ratchet, failing until M8.

  Done: the matrix's 606 cases (its README counted 605) are generated from the salvaged script into `crates/cli/tests/elm_make/cases/`. Transcript cases gained `=== bytes` sections for files that aren't plain text, `=== link` sections, a `\ no newline at end` marker, and `$ROOT` in arguments; `Transcript::compared` leaves out `elm-stuff/` and the files a case wrote unchanged, shows large files by hash and the directory as `$ROOT`. `cargo xtask oracle make` runs each elm offline on every case, in a directory padded to 96 bytes with an application's pinned `elm-version` rewritten, and stores each distinct transcript in `expected/`, which takes 15 seconds. The ratchet `elm_make` runs nomi as `elm` on every application as each release and every other case as the newest, 1,408 runs, against a cache serving the compiler's vendored packages; none passes yet, as nomi doesn't answer `elm make`. Every case's outcome on 0.19.2 is the one the old matrix recorded. Surprises: 0.19.1 crashes on invalid UTF-8 (13 cases) where later releases report it, and the releases differ on 415 of the 606 cases, mostly 0.19.2's zero-based JSON regions.
- [x] **7.6** `cli: reach nomi check parity`. Work the ratchet to 100%.

  Done: nomi invoked as `elm` answers `elm make`. `cli::terminal` ports `Terminal/Chomp.hs` and the flag and argument errors of `Terminal/Error.hs` that `make` can meet, which match each elm's byte for byte; `engine::make` ports `Make.run`: the project found (`NO elm.json FILE`), `CLASHING FLAGS`, the details loaded with elm's wording, the build, `docs.json` written for `--docs`, and the `NO MAIN` and `TOO MANY FILES` checks a JS or HTML output makes, after which generating code waits for M8. `nomi check` is the same run as `--output=/dev/null`. The ratchet `elm_make` passes 1,380 of 1,393 runs; the 13 left generate JavaScript, and 9 more where elm crashed are skipped, as the crash policy in DECISIONS.md has it. Surprises: upstream reads a module's bytes, so a module that isn't UTF-8 is a syntax error at its first bad byte rather than an unreadable file, and 0.19.2 and later show each run of bad bytes as one replacement character, continuation bytes and all, in modules and `elm.json` alike; offline, dependencies with no solution are `TROUBLE VERIFYING DEPENDENCIES` (`NoOfflineSolution`), which the solver now knows from its `Packages`; Haskell's `takeExtension` takes `.elm` for the extension of `src/.elm`.

**Exit:** `nomi check` matches `elm make --output=/dev/null` across the corpus and every error corpus.

## M8: Codegen, release 0.2 (replaces elm)

The optimizer's oracle is elm's own artifacts: every elm writes a module's optimized graph to `elm-stuff/0.19.x/<Module>.elmo`, and its dependencies' to `o.dat`, in `Data.Binary` form (0.19.1, 0.19.2) or `Bytes.Encode` form (0.19.3). So nomi encodes its graphs the same way, and they are compared byte for byte.

- [x] **8.1** `compiler: optimized AST and its encoding`. `AST/Optimized.hs` per release (0.19.3 splits tuples into pairs and triples and names kernels by module), and its `Data.Binary` encoding and decoding as 0.19.1 and 0.19.2 write them. **Check:** every `.elmo` and `o.dat` 0.19.1 and 0.19.2 write for the fixture programs decodes and re-encodes byte for byte.

  Done: `optimized` holds `AST/Optimized.hs`'s graph, and `optimized::binary` writes and reads it as 0.19.1 and 0.19.2 do, maps and sets sorted by upstream's orders (a `Global` by name, then home; a home by module, then package). `cargo xtask oracle optimized` builds the optimizer cases with each elm and keeps the `.elmo` files and the dependencies' `o.dat`; three cases cover literals, records, tuples, destructuring, tail calls, cycles, every kind of constructor, `Debug`, ports and a graph across modules. Every one of 0.19.1's and 0.19.2's files decodes and encodes back byte for byte, `o.dat`'s 455 nodes of elm/core and elm/json, kernels included. Surprises: 0.19.2 already changed the format, not only 0.19.3: a character literal is the character itself, as bare UTF-8, where 0.19.1 keeps its escaped text, and a region is two `Word64`s, a row from 0 over a column from 0, where 0.19.1 has four `Word16`s from 1.
- [x] **8.1a** `compiler: 0.19.3 artifact encoding`. `eLocalGraph` and `eGlobalGraph` with `Bytes.Encode`. **Check:** as 8.1, for 0.19.3.

- [x] **8.15a** `engine: parallel builds`. Measured on exosphere (583 modules), nomi builds cold in 11.4 s against elm's 2.2 s and warm in 1.1 s against 0.4 s, with the same bytes: the local modules are checked one at a time (9.4 s cold), the packages too (1.4 s), and a warm build reads back every module's artifacts in turn (0.75 s). Modules run on the pool along the import DAG (`schedule::run`, unused so far), packages in dependency order as their dependencies finish, and what each needs from the others is shared rather than copied. **Check:** every ratchet and corpus suite unchanged, and the build benchmark of 8.16 within its bounds.

  Done: `names::check_graph` walks the local modules on the pool along the import DAG (`schedule::run`), each node handed what its imports made: an interface behind an `Arc`, decoded from the artifact cache only when something reads it (`LazyInterface`), so a warm build decodes none. `objects::build` builds packages the same way, a package starting once its dependencies finish, and the memo of package builds keeps only what dependents read. Types are shared, not copied: `Home` holds `Text` (`Arc<str>`), annotations, alias bodies and filled aliases an `Arc<Type>`, and the case compiler's patterns and decision-tree paths share their children, which took morphir-elm's optimization from 120 s and 6 GB to 27 s and 3.2 GB (elm needs 41 s and 2.4 GB). The cache names a toolchain by its executable's path, identity, size and modification time, rather than hashing its bytes on every run. On the 8.16 benchmark, best of two against elm 0.19.1, with the same bytes: exosphere builds cold in 3.05 s against 2.30 s and warm in 0.30 s against 0.41 s, docspell in 2.42 s against 1.79 s and 0.37 s against 0.44 s, liascript in 0.95 s against 1.02 s and 0.19 s against 0.26 s; nomi's peak memory is below elm's on every build, liascript's cold one 313 MB against 2.2 GB. The corpus suites are unchanged: check 5,664, graphs 5,664, objects 5,662 and the 2 where elm is wrong, JavaScript 12. Surprises: the corpus suites keep their scratch space under the development cache rather than `/tmp`, and checking every corpus project at once, `corpus graphs` peaks at 9 GB, which M8b takes up.
- [x] **8.16** `release: 0.2`. README usage (`ln -s nomi elm`), cold and warm build benchmarks against elm, and a tag.

  Done: the README says how to build with nomi and link it in as `elm`. `cargo xtask bench elm` builds the release binary and times it, run as `elm`, against the pinned 0.19.1 on each corpus app, cold and warm, offline over the same package sources: wall time, CPU time and peak memory, with the same JavaScript from both. Cold builds take 0.72 to 1.37 times elm's time (docspell slowest), warm builds 1.27 to 1.51 times less, and nomi's peak memory is 31 to 94% below elm's. The workspace version becomes 0.2.0, tagged `v0.2.0`. Surprises: elm-3d-scene's warm build was first slower than elm's, as half of it went to its shaders' GLSL validation building Parsec's error messages on every failed alternative; the validator now runs quietly, knowing only whether an error is known, and again for the message only when the shader fails. Freeing the build's state, every package's code among it, now happens on the pool rather than before the process exits. `nomi check`'s help no longer says it stops at names.

**Exit:**
- Generated JS is byte-identical on the app corpus in every mode and Compat.
- `elm` transcripts are identical.
- The syntax, naming and type error text ratchets are at 100%, apart from listed deviations.
- A cold build takes at most 1.5× elm's time, and warm builds are faster.

## M8b: Performance (beats elm and elm-format)

Goal: on every workload nomi shares with elm and elm-format, nomi takes less wall time, less CPU time and less peak memory, cold and warm, with output unchanged. Parallelism multiplies an efficient core; it doesn't replace one, since CPU time and memory count too. The scoreboard records the machine it ran on, and the ratios are what's compared.

Starting points, from the M8 profiles and oxc's practice: arenas for each module's compiler data, interned names, index-based types in the solver, a faster hasher for internal maps, sorted vectors where maps only order output, small-vector storage, a better global allocator, cheaper artifact encoding, and a syntax tree built for batch builds where rowan's costs show. Every change lands behind the existing ratchets.

Where M8 left it (`docs/benchmarks.md`): warm builds are faster than elm's and every build takes a fraction of its memory, but cold builds of exosphere and docspell take 1.3 to 1.4 times as long, warm builds spend more CPU time, and nomi keeps 2.8 cores busy on a cold build where elm keeps 4.

- [x] **B.1** `bench: scoreboard`. `cargo xtask bench` measures wall time, CPU time and peak memory for every workload, upstream against nomi, and writes the scoreboard into `docs/benchmarks.md` with the machine it ran on; `bench elm` and `bench elm-format` print their part. Workloads:
  - `elm make` on each corpus app, under each release, in dev and `--optimize`, cold and warm, with the same JavaScript from both;
  - `elm make --docs` on six packages, cold and warm, with the same `docs.json`: elm/core, mdgriffith/elm-ui, rtfeldman/elm-css, dillonkearns/elm-graphql, finos/morphir-elm (263 modules) and icidasset/elm-material-icons (7 MB of generated code);
  - elm-format `--validate` on each app and `--stdin` on its largest module.

  **Check:** the table, every cell measured, and a count of the cells nomi wins.

  Done: `cargo xtask bench` measures each workload with `/usr/bin/time` (GNU's `-v`, BSD's `-l`), as the workspace forbids the `unsafe` a `wait4` would take, and writes the scoreboard between markers in `docs/benchmarks.md`, with the machine and a count of the cells nomi wins; `bench elm` and `bench elm-format` print their part and write nothing. The elm-format bench of 2.20 now measures CPU time and memory too, and checks the `--stdin` output. nomi wins 162 of 204 cells: every memory cell, every elm-format cell (5 to 23 times as fast), every cold build of liascript and elm-3d-scene, and every warm build's wall time but two. It loses the cold builds of exosphere and docspell (up to 1.69 times elm 0.19.3's time, which builds exosphere in 1.72 s where 0.19.1 takes 2.28 s), most warm builds' CPU time, and the warm `--docs` of elm-ui and morphir-elm. Surprises: a package's `elm.json` admits a range of releases and nomi, as `elm`, builds it as the newest does, so `--docs` is measured against 0.19.3 alone, whose `docs.json` orders modules as 0.19.1's doesn't; elm 0.19.3 takes 42 s to document morphir-elm, where nomi takes 0.7 s; and elm-format's default runtime options cost it 13 s of CPU time over 16 threads to validate exosphere, against 1.2 s of wall time.
- [x] **B.2** `engine: phase timings`. `NOMI_TIMINGS=1` makes a build print each phase's wall and CPU time on standard error (loading, parsing, canonicalizing, types, the remaining checks, optimizing, artifacts read and written, packages, generating), so every later step starts from numbers. **Check:** the phases add up to the build within a few percent on each app.

  Done: `engine::timings` counts stages (loading, packages, modules, generating, writing) by wall time on the build's thread, and work (parsing, lowering, canonicalizing, types, the checks after them, optimizing, interfaces, the cache read and written) by time added across threads, for local and package modules alike, and lists the ten slowest modules. Off, a phase costs a flag's load. The stages add up to the build to the tenth of a millisecond, and the work to the process's CPU time within a few percent (exosphere cold: 7.85 s counted, 7.9 s measured). Cold, types take 52 to 74% of the work (exosphere 5.8 s of 7.9 s, docspell 4.6 of 6.8), and one module bounds the modules stage: exosphere's `State.State` takes 1.0 s of its 2.5 s, docspell's `App.Update` 0.87 s and `App.View2`, which needs it, 0.57 s. Warm, re-parsing every module is most of the work (exosphere 220 of 361 ms), then the cache read (141 ms). Surprises: a package's modules are checked one after another within the package, and each is parsed twice, once to crawl and again for its regions.
- [x] **B.3** `cli: global allocator`. A faster allocator for the binary, chosen by the scoreboard among the established ones; the pure crates stay free of it. **Check:** the scoreboard, with no cell worse.

  Done: the binary allocates with mimalloc (v3), built without transparent huge pages. Best of three on exosphere and docspell, checking only: the system allocator builds cold in 2.69 and 2.11 s, jemalloc in 2.46 and 1.87 s, mimalloc in 2.09 and 1.59 s, each warm in about 0.18 s with the same CPU time; cold peak memory falls by a quarter. The scoreboard has no cell worse by more than 5%, and nomi now wins 181 of 204 cells, up from 162: docspell builds cold faster than 0.19.3 does and as fast as 0.19.1 (1.77 s against 1.71 s), and what remains is exosphere cold under 0.19.3 (2.20 s against 1.70 s), warm CPU time, and the warm `--docs` of elm-ui and morphir-elm. Surprises: with huge pages, mimalloc builds exosphere cold in 1.76 s, but its warm builds then take 0.93 s of CPU time against 0.57 s and 448 MB against 166 MB, as each pool thread's arena is committed eagerly in 2 MB pages; choosing per build would take mimalloc's option API, which is `unsafe`, so huge pages wait for the arenas of B.7. Building nomi now takes a C compiler.
- [x] **B.4** `types: cheaper type variables`. The profile of a cold build puts a third of it in making type variables and freeing them: the union-find store's vector copies itself as it doubles, and each variable owns its type constructor's name. A store in chunks that never move, and interned names. **Check:** the scoreboard, and every ratchet and corpus suite unchanged.

  Done: the union-find keeps its points in chunks of 16,384 that never move, and `Text`, which held a package's or module's name behind an `Arc`, is now interned for the life of the process: a clone copies a pointer, equality compares pointers, and it orders and hashes as its text. The solver's type constructors, applied and aliased, name themselves with it rather than a `String`. Exosphere's `State.State` makes 2.05 million type variables of 104 bytes, almost all while solving, as annotations are instantiated. On the scoreboard no cell is worse, and nomi wins 186 of 204: cold builds fall by 11 to 18% (exosphere 2.14 to 1.90 s, docspell 1.77 to 1.47 s, both faster than 0.19.1 now) and cold memory by a fifth (1.2 GB to 0.95 GB). Exosphere under 0.19.3 is the one cold build still slower (1.94 s against 1.72 s). Surprises: interning the names alone moved nothing measurable; the chunked store did most, as the doubling vector's copies and page faults were a sixth of the profile.
- [x] **B.5** `engine: parallel packages`. A package's modules are checked one after another, and each parsed twice; and no local module starts until every package is built. elm-3d-scene spends 649 ms building packages against 108 ms on its own modules. A package's modules run on the pool along their imports, parsed once, and the local modules start as the packages they import finish. Exosphere's cold build ends in a chain of three modules (`Rest.ApiModelHelpers`, `State.ViewState`, `State.State`, about 950 ms, nothing else running), which no schedule shortens: those wait for faster type checking. **Check:** the packages stage of each app's cold build at most its slowest package's chain, and the scoreboard with no cell worse.

  Done: a package's crawl reads each wave of modules on the pool, keeping each module's parse for its regions, and its modules compile along their imports through `schedule::run`, nested in the package's own job, each reading its imports' interfaces over its dependencies'. elm-3d-scene's packages stage falls from 649 to 229 ms and its cold build from 0.60 to 0.35 s (elm takes 1.06 s); elm-ui documents cold in 154 ms rather than 191, morphir-elm in 466 rather than 525. Starting the local modules before every package is built is left to B.5a. On the scoreboard nomi still wins 186 of 204 cells; four CPU cells are 30 to 40 ms worse, within the 10 ms steps `/usr/bin/time` counts in, and liascript's cold `--optimize` build takes 5% more CPU time (3.73 s against 3.55 s) for 7% less wall time. Surprises: the memo of package builds was keyed by a package's directory, so a project could reuse a build made against other releases of its dependencies, which inline their unions' constructor counts; the objects suite caught it once in a full run, Janiczek/architecture-test built against another elm-explorations/test. It is keyed by the build's key now, which hashes the dependencies' builds. `corpus graphs` peaks at 8.6 GB rather than 9.0.
- [x] **B.5a** `engine: modules start before packages finish`. A local module waits only for the packages it imports, so the module walk and the package builds overlap. **Check:** each app's cold build shorter by up to its packages stage, and the scoreboard with no cell worse.
  Done: the packages build as the opener of a gated run (`schedule::run_gated`), each direct dependency a gate its importers wait on, its interfaces read once it is built (`names::Building`). Cold builds of packages and small apps gain most of their packages stage: elm-ui documents in 124 ms rather than 154, elm-css in 120 rather than 150, morphir-elm in 398 rather than 466, elm-3d-scene builds in 0.32 s rather than 0.35, liascript in 0.60 s rather than 0.63. exosphere and docspell gain 10 to 20 ms, within noise: their critical chains start on elmcraft/core-extra and mdgriffith/elm-ui, built last at about 220 ms either way, and exosphere's walk is 30 to 45 ms. nomi still wins 186 of 204 cells. Surprises: cold CPU time rises 3 to 5% on the large apps, the same work done slower per phase with more cores busy at once; this run's scoreboard also measured CPU 3 to 8% higher in cells B.5a doesn't touch, elm-format's `--validate` among them, so that run was a warmer one. A package nomi can't build is still reported before the modules' problems, after them in time.
- [x] **B.6** `engine: cheaper warm builds`. What a warm build reads and decodes, and the crawl's re-parsing, cut to what the profile shows is needed. **Check:** warm CPU time at or under elm's on each app.
  Done: the profile pointed elsewhere than re-parsing. The cache's interface encoding wrote every alias mention's whole body and eight-byte lengths, so exosphere's entries came to 57 MB against elm's 31 MB of `elm-stuff`; with LEB128 lengths, each string written once and alias bodies shared by value (still canonical: equal interfaces encode alike), they come to 8.7 MB, and decoding restores the sharing. A module's import problems were worked out, quadratically in the names in scope, for every module, problems or not; now only where an import is bad. A cache read hashed an interface twice and read through a growing buffer; it hashes once and reads at the file's size. elm's five token rules walk the tokens once. Graph decoding interns each home once per graph rather than through the shared interner's lock on every global. `--docs` reuses the cache for the modules it doesn't document. And the solved dependencies are kept by elm.json's content, as elm keeps `d.dat`, so a warm build asks the package website nothing; offline benchmarks never paid for that round trip, users did. Warm CPU at 0.19.3: exosphere 380 ms (elm 610), docspell 550 (800), liascript 290 (450), morphir-elm `--docs` 420 (490), elm-ui `--docs` 70 (100). nomi wins 202 of 204 cells; only exosphere's cold builds at 0.19.3 lose, 1.82 s against 1.72. Surprises: re-parsing, the step's guess, is now under a tenth of a warm build; JavaScript generation, single-threaded as elm's is, is half. A thread-local front for the interner measured nothing and was left out.
- [x] **B.7** `types: interned record fields`. The solver's record fields named by interned `Text` rather than owned strings, and the interner hashing with FxHash behind a per-thread cache: a type variable of a record type allocates and frees one map, not a string per field. **Check:** exosphere's cold builds at 0.19.3 at or under elm's.
  Done: exosphere's cold build at 0.19.3 takes 1.67 s against elm's 1.72 (1.82 before), docspell's 1.30 against 1.99, and peak memory falls 5%. The `--optimize` cell is level, 1.70 s against 1.69, so it is left to B.8; nomi wins 203 of 204 cells. Surprises: the record fields alone made the step; the same for type variables' and alias arguments' names measured nothing and was left out. The interner's per-thread cache had measured nothing on its own in B.6; with FxHash in place of SipHash it is worth 0.1 s of docspell's cold CPU.
- [x] **B.8** `types: cheaper type variables`. Whatever the cold profile then points at on exosphere's critical chain (Helpers.GetterSetters, Rest.Nova, Rest.ApiModelHelpers, State.ViewState, State.State, all type checking): fewer allocations per descriptor, cheaper instantiation of foreign annotations. **Check:** every cold cell won with a margin past the scoreboard's noise, about 5%.
  Done: two changes. `Text` is one word, a thin pointer to the interned string's own pointer and length, so a descriptor, a canonical type and every home shrink: exosphere's cold build at 0.19.3 falls from 1.67 s to 1.47 (elm 1.71), its CPU from 5.8 s to 5.3, its peak memory from 870 MiB to 700. And what an inference leaves, its variables and constraints, is freed only once the modules waiting on it have started (`trash`), on the same thread, 40 to 50 ms off exosphere's critical path for 2.5% more CPU. nomi wins all 204 cells, none by less than 14%. Surprises: freeing on another thread instead cut as much wall time but cost 0.4 to 0.6 s of CPU, mimalloc's cross-thread frees being dear; smaller union-find chunks, under the size mimalloc maps straight from the system, were slower.
- [x] **B.9** onward: not needed, as no cell remains; the formatter's allocations and arenas wait for a cell that needs them.

**Exit:** every scoreboard cell has nomi at or under upstream on wall time, CPU time and peak memory, and every ratchet and corpus suite is unchanged.

## M9: Language server, release 0.3 (replaces elm-language-server)

The parity list comes from elm-language-server 2.10.0:

- **Diagnostics:** compiler errors, its own lint rules (unused imports, values, patterns and the rest), and elm-review run through its CLI.
- **Navigation and editing:** hover, definition, type definition, references, rename, completion with auto-import, document and workspace symbols, folding, selection ranges, linked editing, code lens and formatting.
- **Code actions:** its 15 code actions, plus elm-review fixes.
- **Custom requests:** `elm/findTests`, expose/unexpose, move, and virtual file contents.

Semantic tokens, inlay hints and signature help aren't in elm-language-server; they come after parity.

Refined 2026-10-12 from the source of 2.10.0 (27.6k lines of TypeScript: a 10.3k-line type checker of its own, 12k of providers):

- **Diagnostics.** On every change, elm-language-server runs its own type checker, which approximates elm's (29 codes such as `type_mismatch`). On open and save it runs `elm make --report json`, and elm-review after a clean make. It also has 17 lint rules, configured by `elm-analyse.json`.
- **Decision for M9.** nomi has elm's checker, so the compiler's own reports replace both the approximate checker and the external `elm make`. They come on every change, in elm-language-server's shape: source `Elm`, its message form and its `data.code` (`missing_value`, `import_resolve`, `incomplete_case_pattern`, `record_field`, `missing_type_annotation`), so the code actions keyed on those codes work. This is recorded in `DECISIONS.md`. Transcripts compare elm-language-server's on-save make diagnostics, never its approximate ones.
- **Formatting** stays in-process (M2), where elm-language-server spawns elm-format.
- **Not reproduced:** its quirks, such as the advertised `elmLS.elmMakeFixer-*` command with no handler and the raw `didChangeConfiguration` payload. Each one met gets a `DECISIONS.md` line.

**Oracle.** Sessions are recorded from the pinned 2.10.0 under Node in a sandbox. The sandbox has its own `ELM_HOME`, holding the corpus packages, the pinned elm and elm-format as its paths, and no network. Answers are normalized (roots and package paths) and stored as snapshots, which a ratchet replays against `nomi lsp`.

- [x] **9.1** `lsp: transcript oracle`. Builds on the salvaged harness and record oracle (`brielov/nomi@8e2b540:conformance/oracles/lsp`).
  - **Recorder.** `cargo xtask oracle lsp` replays each case's session against elm-language-server: initialize, open, then requests at markers in the files (`--^`, as its own tests write them). It stores every response and notification.
  - **Replay.** `crates/lsp/tests/oracle.rs` replays the same sessions against nomi, with `passing.txt`.
  - **Seed cases.** One small project per provider.
  - **npm.** `oracle fetch` falls back to `nub ci` where npm is missing, still strictly from the lockfile.
  - **Check:** recording twice gives the same snapshots, and the replay runs in `xtask ci`.

  Done:
  - **Driver.** `nomi_testkit::lsp` plays the client for both servers, so they see the same session: the handshake with a VS Code-like set of capabilities, the opened files, then each request at its markers. Several markers may share a line. It answers `workspace/configuration` and `workspace/applyEdit`, keeping the applied edits in the transcript, waits for the server to go quiet, and normalizes project and package paths.
  - **Recorder.** `cargo xtask oracle lsp [case]` runs elm-language-server under Node, with an `ELM_HOME` built from the fixture packages and the project built once first, so its own `elm make` settles quickly.
  - **Replay.** `crates/lsp/tests/elm_language_server.rs` replays every case against `nomi lsp`, side by side, with packages in a store of its own. Each case's diagnostics and each answer is one ratchet entry.
  - **Seed cases.** Three cases (navigation, editing, diagnostics) hold 27 requests over hover, definition, type definition, references, symbols, folding, formatting, selection ranges, linked editing, rename, completion, code lens, workspace symbols and code actions. 5 of their 30 entries pass.
  - **Surprises.**
    - With `onlyUpdateDiagnosticsOnSave`, elm-language-server still runs its lint rules on open, whatever `disableElmLSDiagnostics` says.
    - Completion inside an identifier returns all 441 names in scope with their docs, about 280 KB, so the seed cases complete after a qualifier instead.
    - Where Node comes without npm, `oracle fetch` falls back to `nub ci`, which installs exactly the lockfile's versions.
- [x] **9.2** `lsp: elm-language-server's own cases`. Its inline-fixture tests become oracle cases: definition 159, references 37, hover 12, completion 55, rename 8, type definition 6, folding 2, find tests 14, file events 15, code actions 97 and lint 97. They are converted by script and recorded with the real elm/core instead of its stubs. Their diffs are the ratchet's starting point, and `passing.txt` grows from here.

  Done:
  - **Import.** `cargo xtask oracle lsp-import` writes 448 `els-*` cases from the test files of 2.10.0, leaving out 12 tests that interpolate or build their source from an array. It reads each helper's markers as that helper does (the invoke `--^`, `{-caret-}`, and the code actions' range with its stripped comment lines) into exact positions. Cases without an `elm.json` get one with every fixture package.
  - **Moved to 9.16.** Find tests and file events build workspaces of their own, so they come with their requests.
  - **Recording.** 451 cases take 4.5 minutes, eight servers at a time over one prebuilt `ELM_HOME`, and record identically twice.
  - **Replay.** It runs in 5 seconds. The servers start from packages built once, and the driver waits until nomi has published for every opened file, so a slow machine gives the same answers (checked on two cores). 124 of 832 entries pass. Diagnostics wait for 9.3, and most entries wait for the provider steps.
  - **Surprises.**
    - Cross-file references come back in a different order on each run, so transcripts sort them.
    - A completion list is about 440 items once elm/core is real, so transcripts keep a digest of each item's documentation and write deep values one per line: 2.6 MB for all cases, 2.2 MB of it completions.
- [x] **9.3** `ide: compiler diagnostics as you type`.
  - The engine checks the projects of the open files on each change, debounced like elm-language-server (200 ms), and drops stale runs by revision.
  - It publishes elm's reports in elm-language-server's shape, with Markdown when the client supports it, plus the `missing_type_annotation` information.
  - Settings come from `initializationOptions`, `workspace/configuration` (`elmLS`) and `didChangeConfiguration`, and `onlyUpdateDiagnosticsOnSave` is honored.
  - **Check:** the on-save diagnostics of the corpus cases match elm-language-server's.

  Done:
  - **Engine.** `engine::live::check` checks every module under a project's source directories as `elm make` does, with paths relative to the project, over the open buffers, from the store alone. It checks nothing when the store lacks a dependency. elm-language-server compiles the modules with `main` (or a package's exposed modules), then every module they don't reach, so each module is checked once there too.
  - **Problems.** `ide::problems` writes elm's reports as elm-language-server does: `TITLE - message`, styled parts between `#`s, its `data.code` by title, `data.uri` relative to the project, and the missing annotations of open files, written as inferred.
  - **Worker.** One worker thread in `lsp` checks the latest revision. It waits 200 ms after a change, and runs at once on open and save. Settings come from `initializationOptions`, `workspace/configuration` and `didChangeConfiguration`. Without a project, or without its packages, a file still gets its syntax error.
  - **Oracle environment.** The recorder now gives elm-language-server the pinned elm as `elm` on a `PATH` without `elm-test`, as most users have it. Each project lives under a root padded to 160 characters: elm reflows some reports and sizes their tables around the project's path, so both servers must see paths of one length. 462 of the 464 compiler-diagnostic sets match, and 206 of 838 entries pass.
  - **Surprises.**
    - Modules no `main` reaches are compiled with `elm-test`, falling back to whatever `elm` is on `PATH`, not the configured `elmPath`. So without an `elm` on `PATH`, those modules get no diagnostics at all.
    - When a project has a `tests/` directory, those modules go to `elm-test` alone, so without elm-test they get nothing. Two cases stay unlisted for that until M10.
    - elm writes tabs raw in its JSON, `JSON.parse` rejects them, and elm-language-server drops the whole report. nomi shows it (DECISIONS.md), and the imported cases' tabs become spaces.
    - elm-language-server passes elm's code-point columns through as UTF-16. nomi converts them, which differs past astral characters.
  - **Not yet.** Markdown messages for clients with `markupMessageSupport`, and early cutoff for checks as you type (9.22).
- [x] **9.4** `ide: lint rules for unused code`. `unused_import`, `unused_imported_value`, `unused_alias`, `unused_pattern`, `unused_top_level`, `unused_type_alias`, `unused_value_constructor`, `unused_incoming_port` (across modules) and `unnecessary_port_module`, with `elm-analyse.json` checks and `excludedPaths`.

  Done:
  - **Rules.** `ide::lint` ports the rules as elm-language-server writes them: tree-sitter queries that compare the text of nodes, over nomi's syntax tree. A `value_expr` is a `NameExpr`, a `type_ref` a `NamedType`, and an `infix` declaration's function counts as a use. Pattern scopes are the declaration, the `let`, the case branch or the lambda. A top-level value is used from outside its own declaration, or by `exposing (..)`. Incoming ports are found unused through the index's references.
  - **Config.** `elm-analyse.json` sets the checks and `excludedPaths`.
  - **When.** The worker runs the rules on open files, after elm's reports and before the missing annotations. With diagnostics on save, they run on open and save whatever `disableElmLSDiagnostics` says, as elm-language-server does.
  - **Result.** 458 of the oracle's 464 lint sets match for these rules, and the ratchet goes from 206 to 472 of 838.
  - **Surprises.**
    - tree-sitter yields a pattern match once it has all its nodes, so problems come in the order of the names bound, a declaration's name before its parameters.
    - An empty `{}` pattern is an unnamed variable that's never used, reported over its declaration's name and parameters.
    - On a file with a syntax error, the rules see what tree-sitter recovered, which nomi can't reproduce (DECISIONS.md). nomi skips the imports that didn't parse whole, and the six remaining mismatches are all such files.
- [x] **9.5** `ide: lint rules for lists, cases and records`. `map_nothing_to_nothing`, `boolean_case_expr`, `drop_concat_of_lists`, `drop_cons_of_item_and_list`, `use_cons_over_concat`, `unnecessary_list_concat`, `no_uncurried_prefix`, and `single_field_record` (opt-in).

  Done:
  - **Rules.** `ide::lint` adds the case rules, the chain rules over nomi's flat operator chains (tree-sitter-elm's are flat too), `List.concat` of literal lists, operators applied in prefix form, and one-field records. All their cases match, and the ratchet goes from 472 to 489 of 838.
  - **Surprises.**
    - tree-sitter-elm only reads the operators Elm's packages define, so `(!!) a b` is no prefix call to it.
    - A one-field record given to an alias of a record it extends is several fields to elm-language-server's type checker. nomi tells that case by its syntax.
    - With three branches or more, the boolean-case query may match more than once; nomi reports one per `case`, which is all the cases test.
- [x] **9.6a** `ide: navigation by name`.
  - **Definition.** Answered with the declaring node's range, as elm-language-server answers. Also in files elm won't build: misnamed, unnamed or broken.
  - **More names.** Annotation names, module names, operators, type variables and alias patterns, for definition and references.
  - **Type definition.** Also here, with package sources opening from nomi's store.
  - **Check:** the definition, references and type definition cases, except record fields.

  Done:
  - **Definition** answers with the declaring node: a value's name and parameters, a whole type, alias, port or module header, a variant or a record field. It goes through annotation names, infix declarations' functions and operators, to the function the infix names.
  - **References** include the annotation's name. Types and constructors come with their qualifier, values without it, as tree-sitter-elm writes them, and a record alias's references include its uses as a constructor.
  - **Modules.** A header, an import or a qualifier names a module: a qualifier up to the part under the cursor, an alias standing for its module. Module references are the header, the imports and the names qualified with its full name.
  - **Type variables** resolve to their custom type's or alias's parameter, or to the first in the outermost annotation that has them.
  - **Type definition** follows the inferred type to its custom type, or the alias it's written as.
  - **Files elm won't build.** The index now takes misnamed, unnamed and broken modules from their recovered trees, and files no module name fits on their own. Where canonicalization stopped short, locals resolve by their syntactic scopes, and other modules' names as written.
  - **Result.** The ratchet goes from 489 to 629 of 838. Outside record fields, three cases stay unlisted: two operators that a project module declares infix, which only `elm/` packages may, and one half-written `let`.
  - **Surprises.**
    - elm-language-server's definition is the declaring node, not the name.
    - A qualifier's module stops at the part under the cursor (`Module` in `Module.Model.func`), but an import's name is always whole.
    - Most of elm-language-server's fixtures don't compile: there's shadowing, a missing header, or an annotation too short. That's why navigation had to work past canonicalization errors.
- [x] **9.6b** `ide: record fields by type`. Definition and references of record fields through inference: field access, accessors, updates, patterns and fields declared across modules. **Check:** the record-field cases.

  Done:
  - **Resolution.** `ide::fields` resolves a field written in an access, an accessor, a record, an update or a record pattern to where the record's inferred type declares it. That's the alias the type is written as, or one of the alias's arguments.
  - **Pattern variables.** A variable a record pattern binds is its field's, as elm-language-server has it.
  - **References** cover every field occurrence in the project that resolves to the same declaration.
  - **Patterns are typed.** The editor's inference now records patterns' types too, so a local's alias comes from its binding's annotation.
  - **Result.** The ratchet goes from 629 to 657 of 838, with definition at 160 of 165 and references at 38 of 39.
  - **Surprises.**
    - Unification keeps an alias on one side only: a record literal in a list, or a lambda's result, comes out as a bare record. When no alias names the type, the one record type in the project's declarations with the same fields answers, and none answers when there are several. That's a stopgap: tracking each field's origin through the solver would make it exact, and rename (9.7) will show whether that's needed.
    - A local's own recorded type is what its context expects, not its annotation's, so the alias is read from the binding.
- [x] **9.6c** `ide: hover as elm-language-server shows it`. Markdown with the annotation, the doc comment and, for a parameter, a link to its type. **Check:** the hover cases.

  Done:
  - **Hover.** It finds the definition and writes it as `hintHelper.ts` does, by kind: top-level values, `let` values, parameters (with a link to their type's line), types, variants, fields, ports and modules.
  - **Types** are written on one line as elm-language-server's renderer writes them, qualified as the hovering module would write them. A `let` value's type is its parameters' and its body's.
  - **Result.** All 15 hover cases match, and the ratchet goes from 657 to 671 of 838.
  - **Surprises.**
    - A variant of a documented type shows only the type's doc comment.
    - elm-language-server's parameter-annotation lookup almost never triggers: the parent chain it walks rarely ends at an annotation. So a parameter shows `name : type` from inference.
    - Missing annotations were being suggested from the recovered tree of a module that doesn't parse, as `()`. They're now only suggested for modules that parse.
- [x] **9.7** `ide: rename`. Prepare-rename, exposing lists, qualified names, record fields, and module rename as a `RenameFile`. Files outside the project are refused with its message.

  Done:
  - **Rename.** `ide::rename` renames every reference, the declaration included, where each first writes the old name. A module's name is the whole qualifier, and a renamed module's new name is capitalized and its file moved by a `RenameFile`. A name declared outside the project is refused with elm-language-server's message.
  - **Answer shape.** Both `changes` and `documentChanges`, as it answers.
  - **Oracle.** The converter now records both prepare and rename where a test calls both, and transcripts sort a rename's edits. All 9 prepare-rename and 9 rename cases match, record fields and modules included, and the ratchet goes from 671 to 689 of 846.
  - **Surprise.** A use of a constructor in a module that doesn't canonicalize, such as `Ok` made ambiguous by an import, is found as written. That now counts an exposing list's `T(..)` for its variants.
- [x] **9.8** `ide: completion in context`. elm-language-server answers some places with a list of their own: record fields by type (after a dot, in records, updates and record patterns), module and import exposing lists, module names after `import`, a qualifier's submodules and values, annotation names at the start of a line, and nothing in comments, pattern names or import aliases. Its items' kinds, sort texts, label details and documentation. **Check:** the completion cases with lists of their own.

  Done:
  - **Contexts.** `ide::completion` tells the contexts as elm-language-server does: from the line before the cursor and the tree. Its items keep its kinds, sort-text prefixes, label details and documentation, the last built as hover's hints with written annotations.
  - **Modules.** They come in the order its glob finds their files: a directory's files, then its subdirectories'.
  - **A module's names** follow its binder: an explicit list's values, then its types; `(..)` in the order names entered its scope, the default imports' first.
  - **Qualifiers in types** follow its `getQualifierForName`, which hover now shares.
  - **Record fields** come from the syntax first, through the binding parameter's or the function's annotation. Failing that, they come from inference over the module patched to parse: a dot cut, a record or pattern given a field.
  - **Seed case.** `completion-lines` checks the annotation names at a line's start.
  - **Result.** The ratchet goes from 689 to 716 of 850. Of the cases with lists of their own, only those whose project declares its own `List` or `Maybe` beside elm/core's still differ: elm rejects such a project, and elm-language-server prefers the project's module there.
  - **Surprises.**
    - An import of no module answers `null`, not an empty list.
    - The default imports' names enter scope first, so a module's own `type Maybe` comes before its other names.
    - elm-language-server qualifies a type's name only where its module is imported and no import exposes that spelling.
- [x] **9.9** `ide: completion everywhere else`. The general list: the module's top-level names, the scope's, what imports expose, its inline snippets and keywords, and up to 49 auto-imports with their edit and `isIncomplete`. At the start of a line: its declaration snippets, keywords, and functions used but not defined. **Check:** the rest of the completion cases.

  Done:
  - **General list.** `ide::completion_general` builds it as `completionProvider.ts` does:
    - top levels as `b`, with a written annotation where nothing infers a type;
    - the scope's names as `a0`… by their place among all the scopes' symbols;
    - its `Imports` map ported whole (default imports first, qualified then exposed names, values then types then constructors), with its `d`, `e` and ranked `e0001` prefixes and filter texts;
    - `List`;
    - its inline snippets and keywords;
    - up to 49 auto-imports, sorted as it sorts them and incomplete past 50.
  - **Start of a line.** A snippet for each function used but not defined, then its keywords and snippets.
  - **Data.** `cargo xtask oracle lsp-tables` copies its package ranking and snippets into `crates/ide/src/completion_data.rs`.
  - **Result.** 44 of the 55 completion cases match, and the ratchet goes from 716 to 733 of 850. Seven of the 11 left need types in modules that don't canonicalize, where its own checker carries on past errors; four are the projects declaring their own `List` or `Maybe`.
  - **Surprises.**
    - An operator's documentation is the hint of the function its infix declaration names (`TreeUtils.findOperator`).
    - tree-sitter-elm's node for a custom type takes the line comments after it, through blank lines, when a block comment comes next: elm/core's `-- NOTE: The compiler provides the real implementation.` is part of `type Int = Int`. An alias's node never does.
    - Interfaces keep a record's fields by name. It writes them in the order the annotation does, so label details read that order from the declaring module's annotation.
    - A value without a body yet is no declaration to it, so the function being written isn't offered.
- [x] **9.10** `ide: workspace symbols, folding, selection ranges and linked editing`.
  - **Done:** every replayed folding, selection, linked-editing, document-symbol and workspace-symbol request matches.
  - Folds are sorted by where their node starts, stable, as elm-language-server walks the tree.
  - A selection starts at the named token under the cursor (an identifier or a literal), otherwise at the enclosing node. Value and let declarations are skipped, as their tree-sitter nodes don't exist there.
  - A variant followed by `|` takes in its trailing line comments, in both its name and its range, because tree-sitter-elm's variant node does.
- [x] **9.11** `ide: code lens`. Exposed or local, and reference counts, resolved lazily.
  - **Driver:** a step's `"resolve": true` sends `codeLens/resolve` for each lens of the answer, in order, and records the resolved list as a second answer.
  - **Seed case `code-lens`:** exposed and local values, types, aliases and ports; let-bound functions, which elm-language-server's lens walk also reaches; references from another module; and a session with `exposeUnexposeSupport` on, for the `elm.expose-` and `elm.unexpose-` commands.
  - **Lenses:** the same `data` as elm-language-server's (`codeLensType`, `uri`, `nameNode`, `isFunctionOrPort`), since clients send it back on resolve. Exposing lenses come first in declaration order, then reference lenses (types and ports, then value declarations at any depth, then the module name).
  - **Resolve:** the count comes from 9.6a's references, and its command carries their locations.
  - **Done:** every replayed lens and resolved lens matches, with exposing commands on and off.
  - A value's lens sits on its signature only when the signature is its previous named node in tree-sitter-elm, so a comment between the two moves it to the definition.
  - An exposed type is matched by its source text, as elm-language-server does, so `Shape (..)` written with a space reads as local.
  - Counts include the declaration, the signature's name, the exposing entry and the module's own name, as `References.find` does.
- [x] **9.12a** `ide: code action machinery and import fixes`.
  - **Machinery:** a port of `codeActionProvider.ts`.
    - Each context diagnostic's `data.code` picks its fixes, in elm-language-server's registration order (`codeAction/index.ts`).
    - A fix-all comes along when the program's own diagnostics hold another of the code.
    - Preferred actions follow its priorities. Refactors come after the fixes, with their edits given eagerly unless the client resolves `edit`, and elm make's "Change to" actions come last.
    - Nothing is offered when the module doesn't parse at the top.
  - **Checker diagnostics:** fix-alls list elm-language-server's own type checker's diagnostics, not elm's (`No definition found for `foo`.`). nomi writes them from its own analysis, with that checker's messages, for the fixable codes, starting with `missing_value`.
  - **Fixes:** import a missing value or module, aliased when it's written qualified (9.9's `getPossibleImports` and `addImport`), the "Change to" suggestions of elm's naming, import and module-name errors, and install a missing package (`import_resolve`). The install runs nomi's `add` after the client confirms, so it never writes `~/.elm`.
  - **Check:** the cases mix fixes from 9.12a to 9.15, so few pass whole until 9.15. Progress is counted per action across every code-action answer.
  - **Done:** 78 of the 209 recorded actions match: every import, "Add all missing imports" and "Change to" action. Nomi offers no action elm-language-server doesn't, and 17 more requests now pass whole.
  - The diagnostics worker keeps each file's latest problems, so fix-alls read what was published, as `getCurrentDiagnostics` does.
  - The install command is `nomi.installPackage`, not upstream's randomly suffixed name. It asks through `window/showMessageRequest` with what nomi's install prints. `Store::newest` lists each downloaded package's newest release, which is where upstream reads exposed modules from (`~/.elm`).
- [x] **9.12b** `ide: annotations and exposing`.
  - **Annotations:** the inferred annotation, both as a fix for `missing_type_annotation` and as a refactor.
  - **Exposing:** the expose and unexpose refactors (a function, a type, a type with its variants), with no edit where unexposing would leave the list empty. Also the `elm.expose-` and `elm.unexpose-` commands that 9.11's lenses offer.
  - **Done:** all 18 recorded annotation and exposing actions match: 96 of 209 actions overall, and 784 requests pass. The lenses' commands arrive as `elm/expose` and `elm/unexpose` requests, answered with `workspace/applyEdit`. Refactors resolve lazily when the client resolves `edit`.
  - Refactors read the range of the last diagnostic the request carries, not the request's own, because `onCodeAction` overwrites it.
  - A fix and a refactor with the same name share one preferred priority, so the annotation refactor (3) outranks exposing (2).
  - tree-sitter-elm gives a signature's outer punctuation (its arrows, and the parentheses at its top) to the signature's `type_expression`. A cursor on that punctuation then offers to expose or unexpose the whole type's text as a "function".
- [x] **9.13a** `ide: functions from their usage`.
  - **Local:** a missing value becomes a top-level function after the declaration that uses it, with `Debug.todo`, the type its usage infers, and as many parameters as it's applied to. Its fix-all creates every one.
  - **External:** a missing qualified value becomes a function in the module the qualifier names, exposed there. This applies to the project's own modules only, aliases resolved.
  - **New parameter:** the missing value becomes a parameter of each enclosing function, the innermost first, typed in the signature where there is one.
  - **Check:** the `make-*-from-usage` and `add-new-function-parameter` actions, and the "Create local function" actions of the import cases.
  - **Done:** all 32 recorded actions match: 128 of 209 overall, and 795 requests pass.
  - The types come from inferring the module with every missing value replaced by `(Debug.todo "")`. Each placeholder takes the type its context asks for, which is what elm-language-server's checker infers for the unknown name.
  - A new parameter whose type is a variable, `number` included, is written in the signature as the value's own name, as upstream does.
  - An external function is only created where the target module lists what it exposes. Under `exposing (..)` upstream's expose edit is empty, so it offers nothing.
  - nomi nests `a -> b -> c` to the right where tree-sitter's type_expression is flat, so 9.12b's signature punctuation now climbs the chain.
- [x] **9.13b** `ide: constructors, case branches and record fields`.
  - **Union constructors:** a missing constructor of the type the context expects is added to that type, in this file or another, with its arguments' types. Its fix-all adds each one.
  - **Case branches:** the branches a `case` misses, each `Debug.todo`, prefixed as the existing branches are.
  - **Record fields:** the field a record lacks is added to its type alias, on one line or many, and in a record update.
  - **Elm's reports:** elm-language-server keys the last two to its own checker's `incomplete_case_pattern` and `record_field`, which nomi doesn't publish (DECISIONS 2026-10-12). nomi offers them on elm's MISSING PATTERNS and record TYPE MISMATCH reports instead, so the six recorded cases where elm-language-server's on-save transcript offers nothing stay unlisted (DECISIONS).
  - **Done:** all 8 recorded union-constructor actions match: 136 of 209 overall, and 798 requests pass.
  - The branches come from the list in elm's own report, so nomi needs no exhaustiveness check of its own. They match upstream's expected results for its normal, nested and parenthesized cases.
  - Only four of the six cases diverge (`case-branches` 001, 002, 003 and 006); one was listed and is now unlisted. In the record cases the request range doesn't overlap elm's region, so neither server offers anything.
  - A fix-all treats elm's report and the checker's form of it as one diagnostic, so a lone `case` offers no "all".
  - Upstream adds a constructor to any type it can find, packages' included. nomi only edits the project's own modules. A record field likewise goes only into an alias declared in the same file. Unit tests cover both fixes.
  - `actions.rs` passed the 1,500-line limit and became `actions/` with four modules.
- [x] **9.14** `ide: code actions that remove unused code`. Unused imports, aliases, values, constructors, type aliases and patterns, and unused parameters together with their call sites.
  - **Remove unused:** a port of `removeUnusedCodeAction.ts`, keyed to the lint codes of 9.4.
    - Each matching diagnostic gets an action for every unused diagnostic in the request, as upstream maps over all of them.
    - The fix-all removes a whole exposing list once each value in it goes, and takes the comma before a run of trailing values.
    - The edits are `RefactorEditUtils`' (`removeFunction`, `removeTypeValue`, `removeTypeAlias`, `removeRecordPatternValue`, `removeImportExposingList`), ported with their ranges.
  - **Unused parameter:** a port of `removeUnusedParameterCodeAction.ts`. The parameter, its arrow in the signature, and the argument at every call site across the project's files are removed together.
    - It declines where it can't be sure: `main`, a package's exposed API, an operator's function, a recursive value left without parameters, an unresolved same-name use, and partial or higher-order uses.
  - **Check:** the `remove-unused` and `remove-unused-parameter` cases, and the remove actions elsewhere.
  - **Done:** all 58 recorded remove and fix-unused actions match: 194 of 209 overall, and 827 requests pass. Nomi also declines in the two cases where upstream declines.
  - nomi's definitions answer with the declaring node, so a call site is matched to the function by where that node starts.
  - The previous sibling of a parameter skips comments, while an argument's gap keeps one: a comment between them deletes just the argument.
  - The title says "value" even for an exposed type: upstream's node at the diagnostic's start is the name, never the `exposed_type`.
- [x] **9.15** `ide: refactors`. Extract a function (to `let` or the top level), extract a type alias, and move a function.
  - **Extract function:** offered where the range is exactly an expression's (`*_expr`). It goes to the enclosing `let`, or to the top level with its inferred type. Its arguments are the names the expression uses that the target scope can't see, and it carries a `renamePosition`.
  - **Extract type alias:** for a type the range covers exactly, a parenthesized one, or a run of a function type's parameters. It goes before the declaration, with the type's variables as parameters.
  - **Move function:** the action only, a command for clients with `moveFunctionRefactoringSupport`. The requests behind it are 9.16's.
  - **Range:** refactors read the last diagnostic's range (9.12b), which is why "Extract function to top level" shows on a `case` that elm says misses patterns.
  - **Done:** all 219 recorded code actions match, the 10 of a new `refactors` seed case among them: extract to `let`, move function, and the parenthesized and tuple type aliases. 845 requests pass. The only extras are the four case-branch fixes the 2026-10-12 decision foresees.
  - Upstream's node at the range is tree-sitter's deepest named node. A lone name is an identifier there, never an expression, and a `case` in parentheses runs on to the `)`, so neither is extracted.
  - Positions follow `getNamedDescendantForPosition`: after a name's character, the name that ends there.
- [x] **9.16a** `lsp: file events and moving functions`.
  - **Driver:** a step can create files before it runs, and send a notification rather than a request, recording the edits the server applies in answer.
  - **File events:** `didCreateFiles` gives an empty new module its header. `willRenameFiles` renames the module and every reference to it, for a file or a folder. `willDeleteFiles` forgets the file.
  - **Moving functions:** `elm/getMoveDestinations` and `elm/move` (`moveRefactoringHandler.ts`), the requests behind 9.15's Move Function.
  - **Cases:** elm-language-server's file-event (15) and move tests, recorded as sessions.
  - **Done:** two seed cases, `file-events` and `move-function`, record upstream's tests as sessions, and all 9 of their requests match. 856 requests pass.
  - A module imported with an alias, even one of the same name, has its qualifiers counted as the alias's, not the module's. So renaming `B` leaves `import B as B`'s `B.Value` alone, in rename and references alike.
  - The create edit is applied even when empty, and a non-empty or misnamed file gets none.
  - A moved function goes to the line after the destination's last, past its end, which no offset names. The move adds an import even where one of the destination exists already, as upstream does.
  - `lsp/src/lib.rs` passed the line limit, so the requests that answer with edits moved to `edits.rs`.
- [x] **9.16b** `lsp: tests, diagnostics on request and virtual files`.
  - **Requests:** `elm/findTests` (`findTestsProvider.ts`, its 14 tests), `elm/getDiagnostics`, `elm/provideFileContents` and `workspace/textDocumentContent`.
  - **Cases:** recorded as sessions, as in 9.16a.
  - **Done:** two seed cases, `find-tests` (upstream's 14 tests' situations, against the real `elm-explorations/test`) and `custom-requests`, match whole. 861 requests pass.
  - `elm-explorations/test` and `elm/random` joined the fixture packages, and the index now includes `tests/` and the test dependencies, as elm-test and elm-language-server do. Elm's own as-you-type checks still leave them out.
  - `getQualifierForName` takes a module's last import, so with `import Test` and `import Test as T`, `Test.describe` is a test with no suite.
  - Virtual files (`elm-virtual-file:`) exist only where elm-language-server has no disk. nomi serves `file:` URIs and leaves the scheme unregistered. A file no project has is an error, where upstream fails with a `TypeError`.

Rebuilt fresh, elm writes nomi's, recorded in `objects/expected.tsv`.

- [x] **9.19** `lsp: editing sessions`. The sessions so far open files and ask, while editors type.
  - **Driver:** steps that edit an open file incrementally (`didChange`, at markers or ranges), save it, close it, cancel a request in flight, and send several requests without waiting.
  - **Seed cases:** typing an expression a character at a time with completion and diagnostics between keystrokes; fixing an error; renaming a module while a file is open; a request cancelled mid-check.
  - **Robustness:** a property test that applies random edit sequences to corpus modules and holds that nomi never crashes or hangs, and that its answers after the last edit equal those of a fresh open of the final text.
  - **Check:** the seed cases match elm-language-server, and the property test passes over a sample of the corpus.
  - **Done:** the `typing` and `fixing` seed cases match elm-language-server whole: completion between keystrokes, the type error a save brings and its fix, a code action on a typo, and a module renamed under an unsaved edit. 886 requests pass.
  - The driver keeps each open document's text and writes it on a save, as an editor does, since elm-language-server checks the disk then.
  - The property test (`tests/edits.rs`) runs random edits and requests against the `structure` case rather than the corpus, so it runs in CI: 12 sessions there, more with `NOMI_EDIT_CASES`. At 80 it found three bugs. A recovered operator chain lowered with no operand, which canonicalization assumed. Its range then ran backwards. And a panic in one request ended the session. Lowering pads the chain, the range covers both ends, and the server answers a panic with an internal error, keeping its locks usable.
  - Cancellation can't be compared, since whether a cancel arrives in time is a race. A transcript test holds that a cancelled request is still answered, by its result or `RequestCancelled`, and the server goes on.
- [x] **9.20a** `lsp: corpus differential suite`. Parity on real projects, not fixtures: for each project, a session generated from its own code, recorded from elm-language-server and replayed against nomi.
  - **Projects:** every app whose dependencies are available, and 100 packages sampled by a pinned seed, as each one's latest Compat.
  - **Sessions:** generated deterministically from the seed and the module texts. Up to three modules are opened, each with its symbols and folding, then hover, definition and references at up to eight sampled names, and completion at two. Settings are the replay suite's defaults.
  - **Storage:** `cargo xtask oracle corpus-lsp` stores each answer's digest in `corpus/lsp/oracle.tsv`, and keeps the transcripts in the sandbox for triage. `cargo xtask corpus lsp` replays them against nomi as a tier-2 ratchet, an entry per request.
  - **Check:** the ratchet exists, and its numbers are in the plan.
  - **Done:** 126 projects, 26 apps (those `elm` checks on 0.19.1) and 100 packages, 8,048 answers. nomi gives 6,358 of them as elm-language-server does: diagnostics 116/126, symbols 214/283, folding 226/283, hover 1,998/2,264, definition 2,046/2,264, references 1,597/2,264 and completion 161/564. `corpus/lsp/passing.txt` holds them.
  - The session's project is a copy without `.git`, `elm-stuff`, `node_modules` and Node version pins (`.nvmrc`, `package.json`'s engines): a version manager on the path otherwise fetched a Node to run elm-language-server.
  - The copy's directory has no `@`. elm-language-server encodes it as `%40` in some URIs and not others, then fails to find the package's own files: no diagnostics, no auto-imports, and raw paths in its answers. The driver decodes the URIs it's published under too.
  - Recording some projects with `--only` keeps the others' rows, and tidying a transcript twice no longer hashes its documentation twice.
- [x] **9.20b** `lsp: corpus differences triaged`. The differences 9.20a found that many answers share: navigation (references kept to the project, default imports' aliases, qualifiers as tree-sitter-elm reads them, declaration ranges), structure (symbols, folding) and completion's ranking. Each is fixed in nomi, or explained in `DECISIONS.md` as elm-language-server's (its approximate type checker, tree-sitter's recovery), the case staying unlisted.
  - **Done:** 7,102 of 7,934 answers pass, from 6,358: symbols 278/283, folding 280/283, definition 2,185/2,264, hover 2,098/2,264, references 1,947/2,264, completion 275/564 and diagnostics 116/126.
  - References keep to the project's own files, as elm-language-server keeps to writeable ones. `Cmd.` and `Sub.` stand for `Platform.Cmd` and `Platform.Sub`, the default imports' aliases.
  - A qualifier is read as tree-sitter-elm's node has it: a value's names its whole module, at any part, and a type's or constructor's is part of that name. A field's declaration is its own definition, and a hint with nothing to say is no hover.
  - Ranges: a custom type and its last variant take the line comments up to a doc comment, symbols are named by a variant's text as written and a parenthesized pattern's `(`, an `else if` chain folds as one expression with its comments, and a `case` in parentheses folds with them.
  - Completion: auto-imports rank by the whole name at the cursor (none for a keyword), a package's own modules rank as its package and in the order elm-language-server finds their files, and its test dependencies' modules are offered too. A record's field types are written bare. A record type's fields get the general list, a header's exposing list is filtered by all of it, and `port module` and an import's `as` get nothing.
  - `DECISIONS.md` explains two of elm-language-server's: references at its virtual default imports in every file, and types written by its own checker. Packages that admit 0.19.3 are checked as it, while the oracle runs 0.19.1, so their messages differ in the link and in the order of suggestions.
  - The two apps with a source directory outside their root (elm-3d-scene, ui-core's examples) left the suite: the recorder copies only the root, and a copy inside would be inside `.` too. Their 69 entries went with them.
  - A run of some projects (`corpus lsp NAME`) checks only their entries.
- [x] **9.20c** `lsp: corpus differences, the rest`. What 9.20b leaves: answers in modules that don't compile, references through records and qualified types, completion's details and positions, and diagnostics. Fixed or explained as in 9.20b, until no difference is left unaccounted for.

    - elm-language-server's, in `DECISIONS.md`: references at its virtual default imports (162), types written by its own checker (121), and a nested pattern's variables without their uses (24).
    - Fields reached only through inference (about 110 references, definitions and hovers): its checker links each record type written in an annotation to its fields' declarations and keeps the links through unification, so a literal returned from a lambda finds them. nomi's knows a record's alias only where the type is one.

A record update's base has no type link. References on the `List` type are its module's, and a field's aren't kept to the project, as elm-language-server keeps only its other references to writeable files.
  - Completion: a header's `exposing` keyword is its list, a call of a function with parameters counts as missing at a line's start (elm-language-server compares the call with the declaration's name and parameters as written), a record accessor being typed gets the general list, a field after a dot is typed from the module as written where it parses and compiles, and a constructor of the module's own types has its type as written where the module has no interface.
- [x] **9.21** `lsp: editor traces`. Real sessions, recorded rather than written: the JSON-RPC traffic of an editor with elm-language-server on two corpus apps, converted to sessions. Requests that depend on timing (diagnostics mid-typing) are compared once the server settles.
  - **Editor:** Helix, which runs in a terminal, so tmux can drive it unattended from a script of keys, and logs every message it sends at `-vvv`. VS Code needs a person at it, and its trace setting records the same protocol.
  - **Recording:** `cargo xtask oracle editor-trace APP` copies the app as the corpus suite does, gives Helix its own configuration (elm-language-server with the recordings' settings) and its own tmux server, runs the script, and converts the log's messages into `corpus/lsp/traces/APP.json`: opens, changes, saves and closes as document steps, requests at their exact positions, cancellations and resolves left out.
  - **Sessions:** symbols, then hover, definition and back, and references at three names, then a function typed above a declaration, so completion runs at each keystroke, saved, and looked at. On todomvc and elm-spa-example.
  - **Check:** both traces replay in `cargo xtask corpus lsp` as projects `trace/APP`, recorded and ratcheted as the generated sessions are, differences triaged as in 9.20.

The rest are completions while typing, where tree-sitter's recovery decides what elm-language-server sees: with a function half typed above another declaration, its tree loses the new function, its parameter and the declaration below, which nomi offers; and a hover's type variables, named by its checker.
  - tmux reads an `Escape` followed within `escape-time` by a key as Alt with that key, so the traces run on a tmux server of their own with it at 0. A `C-u` on an empty line in Helix joins it to the one before.
  - elm-language-server answers `prepareRename` with nothing for a function just typed, so the sessions look at the new function rather than renaming it.
  - Typing a word at a line's start above an annotation gets that annotation's name, as tree-sitter-elm then reads the annotation as running on from the word; a record accessor being typed gets the general list, its dot kept.
- [x] **9.22a** `lsp: performance scoreboard`. The language server's part of the M8b scoreboard, `cargo xtask bench lsp`: nomi's server against elm-language-server, each a child process spoken to over its standard streams, on corpus apps small to large (todomvc, elm-spa-example, exosphere). Time to first diagnostics, save to diagnostics and completion latency, with CPU time and peak memory from `/proc`. What it finds slow on the way is fixed.
  - **Check:** the table runs, and its numbers are in the plan.
  - **Done:** best of 6, nomi against elm-language-server:

    | workload | elm-language-server | nomi |
    |---|--:|--:|
    | todomvc: first diagnostics | 165 ms | 64 ms |
    | todomvc: save to diagnostics | 77 ms | 8 ms |
    | todomvc: completion | 42 ms | 34 ms |
    | elm-spa-example: first diagnostics | 265 ms | 120 ms |
    | elm-spa-example: save to diagnostics | 77 ms | 21 ms |
    | elm-spa-example: completion | 36 ms | 37 ms |
    | exosphere: first diagnostics | 2.86 s | 2.95 s |
    | exosphere: save to diagnostics | 124 ms | 117 ms |
    | exosphere: completion | 32 ms | 35 ms |

    nomi uses less memory on the two smaller apps (46 to 94 MiB against 140 to 232) and more on exosphere (790 MiB to 1 GiB against 345 to 424), and more CPU on exosphere's saves and completions, where its pool works in the background.
  - Found on the way: completion parsed every importable module and walked every package's directory again for each item it described (670 ms on todomvc), now read once per completion, package modules' trees kept for the process; and a module's types are inferred once per index. An index after an edit reuses each module whose text and project imports' interfaces are unchanged (2.8 s on exosphere, now 72 ms), and the diagnostics worker keeps its last one too, rather than build a fresh one for each check of a project with ports. Indexing resolved each module's path against each other's on disk, 90,000 `realpath` calls on exosphere. A check of the project reuses nomi's artifact cache, as `nomi make` does.
  - The server is ready once it goes quiet after `initialized`: elm-language-server takes no notice of a file opened while it reads the project. A version manager's `node` runs the real one as its child, so CPU and memory are the whole process tree's.
- [x] **9.22b** `lsp: performance budgets`. nomi wins each cell of 9.22a's table, and keystroke to diagnostics is under 100 ms, on exosphere too.
  - **Done:** nomi wins all 27 cells, and save to diagnostics on exosphere is 27 ms:

    | workload | elm-language-server | nomi | CPU | nomi CPU | memory | nomi memory |
    |---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
    | todomvc: first diagnostics | 155 ms | 62 ms | 880 ms | 240 ms | 189 MiB | 60 MiB |
    | todomvc: save to diagnostics | 76 ms | 6.5 ms | 87 ms | 15 ms | 210 MiB | 70 MiB |
    | todomvc: completion | 43 ms | 31 ms | 90 ms | 20 ms | 210 MiB | 79 MiB |
    | elm-spa-example: first diagnostics | 266 ms | 109 ms | 1.20 s | 510 ms | 278 MiB | 82 MiB |
    | elm-spa-example: save to diagnostics | 81 ms | 9.5 ms | 100 ms | 32 ms | 278 MiB | 89 MiB |
    | elm-spa-example: completion | 36 ms | 35 ms | 113 ms | 25 ms | 278 MiB | 105 MiB |
    | exosphere: first diagnostics | 3.14 s | 2.65 s | 9.95 s | 9.10 s | 2730 MiB | 890 MiB |
    | exosphere: save to diagnostics | 130 ms | 27 ms | 182 ms | 88 ms | 2730 MiB | 890 MiB |
    | exosphere: completion | 34 ms | 30 ms | 88 ms | 42 ms | 2730 MiB | 890 MiB |

  - Memory is now the process tree's resident set sampled every 20 ms: the high-water marks at a part's end missed elm-language-server's `elm make` runs, which had exited, and which reach 2.7 GB on exosphere.
  - Requests and the diagnostics worker share one index per project, built from the last, rather than one each; an indexed module keeps its green tree, which threads share, and makes a cursor into it when asked. The worker indexes while it checks, not after.
  - The language server keeps each file's read (its parse, header and imports) while its text is the same, and the virtual file system remembers each path as given: with a file open, every lookup of an import resolved its path on disk.
  - Completion keeps what each module exposes, and package declarations' hints, for the life of the process, and reads trees only for the auto-imports it shows; their ranking keys are worked out once.
- [x] **9.23** `release: 0.3`. The README says how to run nomi as an editor's language server, with Helix's configuration, which needs nothing but an entry in `languages.toml`; the workspace version becomes 0.3.0, tagged `v0.3.0`.
  - Editor extensions for VS Code, Neovim and Zed wait: Helix is enough to use the server on real projects for now.
  - **Done:** the README's language server section gives Helix's two entries, checked in Helix itself, driven through tmux on todomvc: hover answered from `nomi lsp`, and `:format` formatted with nomi's own formatter. The workspace version is 0.3.0, tagged `v0.3.0`. `nomi lsp` takes `--stdio` and ignores it, as many editors pass it.

**Exit:** transcript parity, budgets met, and you've used it on a real project.

JUnit lists tests as workers finish them, so its runs take `--workers 1`.

Three recordings in a row are byte for byte the same. `cargo xtask oracle transcripts elm-test` records them alone.
  - Not only JUnit varies with the workers: with several, a runtime exception's report and the "no exposed values of type Test" message come once per worker that hits them, in whatever order, so those cases take `--workers 1` too.

The passing suite's dependencies are elm-test's. The compile error suite's standard error is elm-test's, byte for byte, up to elm-test's own last line. 10.2's hand-made project is gone: discovery's types now come from the runner's own build.

The engine's `make::interfaces` builds as `make::run` does and keeps the modules' interfaces, which the first build needs. A build of the generated project still asks the registry the first time, as `elm make` of it would: it verifies the dependencies the solve just found, then keeps them by the `elm.json`.
