- [x] **10.4** `test: Node workers`. `--seed`, `--fuzz`, filters and the worker count.
  - **Supervisor:** `Supervisor.js` in Rust. It listens on a loopback TCP port, which the wrapped program's `net.createConnection` takes as options where elm-test passes a pipe's path: Rust's standard library has Unix sockets but no Windows pipes. Each worker is `node <program> <index>`; messages are lines of JSON as elm-test's. One loop takes the workers' messages, output and exits in the order they come, keeps results by index and prints them in order once `BEGIN` says how many there are, asks the last worker to finish for the `SUMMARY`, then stops the workers. The exit code is the summary's.
  - **One worker:** elm-test runs the program in its own process then, so an exception loading it shows as its message alone. nomi runs one worker through a small driver that does the same.
  - **Workers' output:** what tests print (`Debug.log`) and what Node prints when a worker fails come through the console, as they come; a worker that fails among several adds elm-test's "unexpected runtime exception" message.
  - **Reports:** the JSON reporter's lines are the workers' own text, kept as it came, so they're `JSON.stringify`'s; the console's are the Elm reporter's strings, with elm-test's header and its Windows substitutions. JUnit's XML is 10.5's.
  - **Options:** `--seed` (by default random, as elm-test picks it), `--fuzz` (100) and `--workers` (the logical cores) reach the runner's `main`; the globs are the filter, and elm-test 0.19.3-0 has no other.
  - **Check:** the fixture suites through the JSON reporter, with several workers, print what elm-test's transcripts do, and exit with their codes; the console reporter's output past elm-test's progress line is the transcripts'; the runtime exception's and no tests' standard error is theirs.
  - **Done:** `nomi_test::supervise::run` runs a built runner's program in Node workers and prints as elm-test's supervisor does. The six fixture suites through the JSON, console and colored console reporters, with three workers where the case names none, and the two no-tests cases, give elm-test's standard output (past its progress line), standard error and exit code; the runtime exception through one worker shows its message alone. `random_seed` and `default_workers` are elm-test's defaults, for 10.6's options.
  - The program no longer holds the supervisor's address: each worker is given the port after its index, so a program built once serves every run.
  - A worker's results come keyed by index, which JavaScript lists in numeric order, so the supervisor sorts them so before keeping them: JUnit prints them in the order they came (10.5).

  - **Check:** new transcripts recorded from elm-test 0.19.3-0, `init` and `install` into an application and a package, and with the package already there; the elm-test transcripts now keep the project's files, apart from `elm-stuff`, so they hold the `elm.json` each leaves.
  - **Done:** `nomi_test::install`. Six new transcripts (`init`, `init_already`, `install`, `install_already`, `install_package`, `install_without_json`), and all 43 match, the `elm.json` each leaves included. The ordered JSON value JUnit needed is now `nomi_test::json`, which also writes `JSON.stringify`'s layout.
  - elm-test's `install` leaves the moved dependencies unsorted: `elm install` in the scratch copy promotes test dependencies the new package needs to the main ones, and moving them back puts them last.
  - elm's install in an application without `elm/json` refuses (MISSING DEPENDENCY), which elm-test passes on; the recording keeps that.
  - `Project.init` doesn't check the source directories; only a run does (`validateTestsSourceDirs`), so `install` works in a project whose `src/` is missing, and `Project::read` no longer checks them.
- [x] **10.6c** `test: --watch`. Runs again as the test source directories and `elm.json` change.
  - **Watching:** as `RunTests.runTests` with `watch`: the console cleared (unless `--no-clear-console`, which prints elm-test's "Rerunning tests" banner instead), a run, then `Watching for changes...`; a change to an `.elm` file in the test source directories (past `elm-stuff` and `node_modules`), to `elm.json`, or `tests/` appearing starts a run, named as elm-test names it (`tests/A.elm changed`, `3 files added/changed`); changes within 100 ms of the first join it, and changes during a run start the next. Errors end a run, not the watch.
  - **How:** nomi polls the watched files' sizes and modification times every 100 ms rather than taking a dependency for the operating systems' file events: a test suite's files are few, and chokidar, elm-test's watcher, polls in places too.
  - **Check:** `elm-test --watch` started on a fixture suite runs it, says it's watching, runs again when a test file changes, naming it, and keeps watching after a run that fails to compile.
  - **Done:** `nomi_test::watch`. `nomi test --watch` on the passing suite runs it, watches, runs again on a change to `tests/Passing/One.elm` with `tests/Passing/One.elm changed` on its progress line, and after a syntax error says `elm make` failed and keeps watching, the console cleared before each run. Unit tests hold the event names and messages, `elm-stuff` ignored, and a directory newly watched holding no changes.
  - A run reads the project afresh, so its test source directories can change between runs; the watcher takes them on as chokidar's `add` does, their files not counting as added.
- [x] **10.7** `release: 0.4`.
  - **Corpus:** the 14 corpus apps with an elm-explorations/test suite, each run by `nomi test` and by elm-test 0.19.3-0 with `--report json --seed 12345 --fuzz 100`, in a copy of the app outside the checkout. `cargo xtask oracle corpus-tests` records elm-test's exit code, counts and a digest of its normalized output in `corpus/tests/oracle.tsv`, its packages downloaded by elm into a sandbox; `cargo xtask corpus tests [ONLY]` runs nomi with the development cache, which the test dependencies are downloaded into, and compares. By hand, as the other corpus suites downloading from the registry are.
  - **Release:** the README's section on `nomi test`, the workspace version 0.4.0, tagged `v0.4.0`.
  - **Check:** every recorded app gives elm-test's exit code and output, and M10's exit holds: output matches elm-test for fixed seeds.
  - **Done:** all 14 suites give elm-test's exit code, counts, standard output and standard error. Seven run, 5,130 tests in all, every one passing: concourse (3,097), obsidian-card-board (1,646), textusm (216), exosphere (121), sanderling (30), gingko (19), nixos-search (1). Seven stop before running, under elm-test as under nomi: five on elm-explorations/test 1.x, which elm-test 0.19.3 refuses; liascript on a test importing 1.x's `Shrink`; azimutt on a source directory its build generates. The README's `nomi test` section; the workspace version 0.4.0, tagged `v0.4.0`.
  - The copies leave out Node version pins (`.nvmrc` and the like), as the language-server suite's do: elm-analyse's `.nvmrc` had the version manager on the path run elm-test on Node 8, which can't parse it.

**Exit:** output matches elm-test for fixed seeds, and the corpus apps' test suites pass.

## MQ: Code quality

Every crate brought up to [architecture.md §13](architecture.md#13-code) before the Node platform builds on it: smaller, with no duplication, no stringly-typed values and pure decisions. Behavior doesn't change, and speed waits for a profiling pass after the milestones; this milestone only makes sure no hot path gets slower. Steps go bottom-up through the crate graph, so a crate's own API is settled before the crates above it adapt.

A second, independent audit of the same code (2026-10-13, kept in `~/.cache/nomi-dev/codex-audit/2026-10-13/`; finding ids such as CF02 below are its) found what the first survey missed: computations made only to be discarded, deep copies, repeated inference, and a few bugs. Its cleanup lands in the steps below, Q.6b for the crates already done; its bugs are the QF steps, which change behavior on purpose and carry their own checks. Larger speed questions it raised (the occurs check's revisits of shared types, shared union metadata, printer rewrites, map and hash choices, the JS tag representation, library replacements for the runner's ordered JSON and globs) wait for the profiling pass, as they need measurement first.

- [x] **Q.1** `quality: lints`. The clippy lints that check §13 mechanically, on for the workspace and their findings fixed; and an `xtask ci` check that no comment names a plan step.

  Done: 37 lints on in `[workspace.lints]`, grouped by the §13 rule each checks: `unreachable_pub` and `unused_qualifications` from rustc, and from clippy the ones for bool-heavy structs and parameters, `Option<Option<_>>`, wildcards matching one variant, parameters taken by value or `&mut` but not used so, `self` unused, `Result`s that never fail, needless clones and collects, and plain-code rules such as `let … else` and `if let` for one-arm matches; an `allow` or `expect` must give its reason. About 800 findings: `clippy --fix` took most, the rest by hand, among them the three §13 shapes the lints found (elm-test's `oldest` and `offline` flags as the engine's `Solve`, the language server's two extended capabilities as one struct, and the optimized-graph decoder's `Option<Option<T>>` as `Option<Maybe<T>>`). `xtask ci` fails on a comment naming a plan step or milestone; 119 did, now rewritten to say what the code does. Surprises: three lints that read as §13 cost more than they give and stay off (closures to method paths names paths such as `std::sync::PoisonError::into_inner` and `rowan::api::SyntaxToken::text` the crates can't reach; `missing_docs` wants about 2,000 comments on fields and variants whose names say enough, so N3 now asks for them only where they don't; and `_` arms over the hundreds of `SyntaxKind`s are the right call, so T5 now says so); and `allow_attributes` would have forced `expect` on test support modules that several test binaries share, each using part of them.
- [x] **Q.2** `quality: base`. The crate every other one builds on, so the helpers the survey found copied across crates land here first and the later steps adopt them (a helper moved here has its callers updated at once; the copies elsewhere go in their own crates' steps):
  - shared: `doc::words`, `labelled`, `hint` and the color helpers public; name order (`compareFast`, written 9 times) moved from the compiler as `Compat::compare_names`, and the region shift and column wrap as `Compat` methods; the stack guard (`stacker::maybe_grow` with constants written about 30 times) as `nomi_base::grow`; one `hex`; one UTF-8 width table; the keyword list and operator set public, with `Next::Keyword` and `Next::Close` as enums; a `lock` that recovers a poisoned mutex; `suggest` gains the ranking its four callers re-implement.
  - its own: FxHash from `rustc-hash` rather than hand-written (rule 9), measured; `Pair`'s tuples, `draw`'s flag and `comma_sep`'s conjunction typed; dead `Bold` and `LineIndex::len`; `fits` and `LineIndex`'s repeated lookups shortened; visibility and doc comments. The JSON escaping shared with project waits for Q.3.

  Done: base gains `Compat::{compare_names, sorted_names, editor_shift, wrap, keep, as_str}`, `grow` and `grow_large` (the two stack-guard sizes in use, 64 KiB/1 MiB and 128 KiB/2 MiB) with `on_core_stack`, `hex`, `lock`, `utf8::char_width`, `suggest::{code_points, sort_texts}`, `doc::{words, labelled, red, green, cyan, blue, dull_yellow}`, `json::escape`, and `Keyword`, `Bracket`, `Conjunction`, `OneLine` and `TwoSnippets` where strings, chars and tuples stood. The compiler's name order moved here with its callers; the copies elsewhere, about 90 sites listed in the step's notes, go in their crates' steps. FxHash comes from `rustc-hash` 2; the dead `Bold` and `LineIndex::len` are gone. Measured against Q.1, best of five: exosphere and docspell build in the same wall time, cold and warm, with 2 to 5% less CPU cold. Surprises: the stack guard comes in two sizes, not one; and the compiler's own `words` split on spaces where base's splits on any whitespace, which its adoption has to check.

- [x] **Q.2a** `quality: testkit`. The test helpers copied across crates (`copy_tree` ×7, padded roots ×4, fixture stores, hex digests, `files_below`, bench timing, `digits_after`, the seeds reader, the releases table), and its own findings: LSP step methods as an enum, `markers` and open files typed, `tidy` split, the mutation operations as an enum, `run`/`run_within` sharing their spawn.

  Done: testkit gains what the crates' tests copy: `copy_tree`, `files_below`, `tree_digest`, `padded_dir` and the recordings' `ROOT_LENGTH`; a `packages` module for the fixture store; a `bench` module for the benchmarks' scaling check and table; the seeds reader; `Transcript::parse`, `digits_after` and `percent_decoded`. The releases table needed nothing: `Compat::ALL` is it. Its own findings: language-server steps carry a `Method`, markers are positions and open files a struct, `tidy` is one match over the method, the mutation operations an enum, and `run`/`run_within` share their spawn; `transcript::encode` was dead. The copies in other crates go in their own steps. Surprises: sharing `hex` means testkit depends on base, a layering change with its own `DECISIONS.md` entry and a check that base never uses testkit; a capturing `Console` and the fixture websites need engine and registry types, so they can't live here.

- [x] **Q.3** `quality: project and registry`. Command names (`"init"`, `"install"`) as an `ElmCommand` enum; `Name`/`Version` parsing from text in one call (14 copies); dead decoders; atomic writes, `latest`/`latest_strict`, `all_deps` and the outdated pair each written once; `verify_in(oldest: bool)` and the tuple variants named; `outline.rs` tests that `reports.rs` already covers.

  Done: elm's commands in reports are an `ElmCommand` through project, registry and the engine's personalities; `Name`, `Version` and module names parse from text in one call, and `FromStr` where elm reads them so; licenses are a table entry and the license suggestion a struct; the dead decoders and their report arms are gone, and `outline.rs` checks only what `reports.rs` doesn't. In registry the solver's search takes an `Order` and keeps one cache of the releases it read, for the solve and its explanation alike; the relaxations are a table; atomic writes, the newest and downloaded listings and the outdated pair are each written once; `registry.rs` is `env.rs`, and the unit tests share one fixture. Both adopt base's words, colors, stack guard, `char_width`, `json::escape` and `hex`, and base gains `Compat::compare_name_bytes` for 0.19.1's module names, which may be bytes that aren't UTF-8. Every ratchet and transcript is unchanged, and the `outline` and `check` corpus suites too. Surprises: the store's listings now skip directories that aren't package names in the language server as the engine already did; the five fixture websites stay apart, since one counts concurrent downloads and a shared one in registry would need registry to dev-depend on itself.

- [x] **Q.4** `quality: syntax`. The lexer, parser, grammar, GLSL and validation: the comma-sequence recovery loop (8 copies) as one helper; the lexer's and GLSL's character classes from `nomi_base::ghc`; validation through the typed views, with the accessors codegen doesn't emit yet; the GLSL parser's `quiet` flag and `Error` states; one grammar test harness, and the benches and fixture tests on testkit's.

  Done: the eight comma-separated sequences recover through one `sequence_tail`; a missing node reports itself (`Parser::missing`); the lexer's and GLSL's character classes are base's `ghc`, behind the ASCII fast paths, which drops `icu_properties` and `stacker` from syntax; validation reads through the typed views, whose generator now emits the token accessors alternatives need, drops the ones nothing calls, and fails on a grammar token without a kind; elm-format's three walks are one, and a shader is parsed once; the GLSL parser's error states and quiet flag are typed; the grammar tests share one harness, and the benches and seeds come from testkit. New tree helpers (`child_token`, `tokens`, `significant_tokens`, `trimmed_range`, `literal_body`) wait for format, ide and the compiler to adopt them. Measured against Q.3: builds the same, cold and warm; formatting the corpus apps' 4,287 modules 0.76 s against 0.82, with 8% less CPU. The `syntax` and `format` corpus suites pass whole. Surprises: one finding, distinct token kinds for what the tree now marks by text, is left out, as the tree format, ide and the compiler read would change with it.

- [x] **Q.4a** `quality: syntax reports`. `elm/` and `elm_format/`, which port elm's and elm-format's parsers for their errors: one phrase macro for `fill!` and `fill_sep(words![…])`, the five "loop or stop" encodings as `ControlFlow` with shared helpers, `Reporter::stuck`/`reserved`, `Cursor`/`Region` from base, and the comment flags nothing reads.

  Done: the elm port's loops are `ControlFlow` through `repeat`, `fold_more` and `comma_until`; the reports' recurring shapes (being stuck, a reserved word, a missing operand, an arrow out of place, the def and declaration trios) are `Reporter` methods, and one phrase macro serves where `fill!` and `fill_sep(words![…])` both did; positions and regions are base's; upstream payloads nothing reads are gone. The elm-format port shares elm's hex scanning, drops the comment flags nothing read, and types its shapes. Both take base's words, colors, `wrap`, `grow_large`, `char_width` and keyword list. 632 lines fewer. Beyond the report ratchets and the mutation suites, two throwaway dumps — every error variant under 22 next tokens, 4 cursors and the three releases, and every parse of the mutation corpus — matched before and after each change. Surprises: elm-format's symbol set keeps the backtick, which GHC counts as a symbol and the lexer doesn't, so it stays its own.

- [x] **Q.5** `quality: format`. Comment slots and positional tree variants as named fields; `ForceMultiline` wherever the bare `bool` stands in for it, and `Delimiters` for `group`'s two flags; the markdown renderer's flags and its 144-line `process_elts`; Haskell's `lines` once; one test support module; `invariant_mismatch` shared with xtask through `FromStr`.

  Done: comment slots and the tree's positional variants have named fields; `ForceMultiline` stands wherever a bare `bool` stood for it; `group` takes `Delimiters`; the Markdown renderer's flags are types and `process_elts` is split; Haskell's `lines` is `split_terminator`; the operator and parenthesis renderers are one each; `upstream_breaks` is a table of named checks; `Invariant` parses with `FromStr` and `invariant_mismatch` is shared with xtask, whose copy is gone; `block` and `structure` live under `render`, and the unit tests share one support module. It takes base's stack guard (dropping `stacker`), syntax's tree helpers and testkit's bench. The Markdown recursion and long `<|` chains are guarded too, and `tests/depth.rs` covers both. Formatted output is byte-identical: a throwaway dump of 36,756 modules (the corpus and every fixture) and 36,456 random mutations of the fixtures matched before and after, and the `format` corpus suite passes whole; formatting the corpus apps takes the same 0.56 s. Surprises: the crate grew by 600 lines, not shrank — named fields, full variant lists and the new tables take more lines than tuples and `_` arms did — while the workspace sheds xtask's copy; a deep link label would make a good depth test but Cheapskate's label parsing is cubic.

- [x] **Q.6** `quality: compiler front`. The top-level modules, `canonicalize` and `hir`: one report context and the `Duplicate*` errors as one variant; alias expansion once (three copies, a fourth in codegen-js); `Home::core`, `Home::is`, `Home::author_project`; the interned `Text` moved to base (§4); positional variants as struct variants, effect kinds as enums; `minimal_cycle` once; `add_import` split; dead `hir` nodes and `Uses.delayed`; the test setup shared.

  Done: the interned `Text` is base's now, as §4 says, moved with its representation unchanged; alias expansion is one set of methods on the canonical types, which the type checker's names and ports use too; `Home::{core, is, author_project}`; the four duplicate-name errors are one `Duplicate { what }`, and `DuplicateBinop`, `NameKind::Pattern`, `Uses.delayed`, the HIR's unread `node` fields and `SourceMap::def` are gone; positional variants in the canonical and optimized trees have named fields, with `Fixity`, `VarType`, `ManagerFields`, `PortDirection` and `MainKind` where strings and flags stood; `minimal_cycle` is the graph module's; `add_import` is split; report types render through `types::render`; the optimized tree's regions are base's, which drops two conversions in the engine; the tests share one setup. Base gains `suggest::nearest`. The scope shrank from 10,625 lines to 10,168. Measured against Q.5: the same, cold and warm, once `Text`'s small methods are `#[inline]`; the `check`, `graphs`, `javascript` and `objects` corpus suites are unchanged. Surprises: moving `Text` across crates cost 1–3% of a cold build until its methods were marked inline, as the workspace builds without LTO; what changes is confined to modules elm rejects (ports in a module that isn't a port module are no longer canonicalized, for the language server's index alone, and a repeated effect field keeps the first) and to messages nothing produces.

- [x] **Q.6a** `quality: compiler back`. `types`, `nitpick`, `optimize`, `optimized`, `interface` and `docs`: one report context in place of four and a `Report` struct for the tuples; `op_left`/`op_right` and `bad_math` with `Side` and `MathOp`; the Cmd/Sub strings as an enum; dead code (`has_debug_uses`, `Expected::map`, `UnionFind::len`); `types` visibility cut to what other crates use; needless clones in unify and solve, measured.

  Done: one `report::{ReportContext, Report}` serves the type, nitpick, `main` and documentation reports, canonicalize's sharing `Report`; `types` is private but for `TypeError`, `Inferred`, `Leftovers` and the modules other crates use; `op_left`/`op_right` and `bad_math` take `Side` and `MathOp`, and the effect strings are an enum; `has_debug_uses`, `Expected::map`, `UnionFind::len`, the never-built `Type::PlaceHolder` with its alias map, and payload fields nothing read are gone; unification's context is `Copy` and its steps move contents rather than clone them, solve and the occurs check copy less, nitpick's patterns borrow their unions, and the optimizer stops deep-cloning list rests; `binary::names` is base's `compare_names`, now `#[inline]`. The scope went from 13,244 lines to 13,056. Measured against Q.6: exosphere builds cold in 1.24 s against 1.28 with 4% less CPU, docspell in 0.91 s against 0.95; the `check`, `graphs`, `objects`, `javascript` and `docs` corpus suites are unchanged. Surprises: the full variant lists §13 then asked for cost about 110 lines, which T5 now spares predicates.

- [x] **Q.6b** `quality: follow-ups in base, project, registry, syntax and format`. What the audit found in crates Q.2–Q.5 already did, each checked against the code as it now is: base's unused diagnostic framework (`Span`, `Code`, `Severity`, `Diagnostic`, `Label`, `Fix`, `Edit`) deleted rather than adopted, since upstream's reports and the editor's problems are different shapes, with a `DECISIONS.md` entry (FND-01); the decoder's arbitrary alternatives as the one two-case choice they serve (FND-08); outlines, release maps and the registry index changed in place rather than cloned to look things up (FND-02/03/05/06); digests streamed into their hasher (FND-04); the parser's repeated lookahead on tuple-constructor commas and outdented import lists (SYN-01); the formatter's cloned inline vectors and the layouts it builds only to drop (FMT-01/02/04–10/16). Measured against Q.6a.

  Done: base's unused diagnostic model is gone, with §4 and a `DECISIONS.md` entry saying what holds instead (upstream's reports in base, the editor's problems in ide); outlines are cloned only when a promotion happens, adding a package is the outline plus one insertion rather than a diff replayed, the registry index updates in place and says how many releases it added, the solver moves each solved release's dependencies out, and decoding `exposed-modules` tries the list and then the sections without a combinator of arbitrary alternatives; mutation and tree digests stream into their hasher; the parser's tuple-constructor and outdented-import recognizers scan forward once (`Parser::scan`), so 50,000 commas no longer take 3.55 s; the formatter consumes its inline vectors, decides layouts before building them, and borrows comments; and the smaller cleanups beside them (FND-09 to 12, SYN-02 and 04 to 07, FMT-11 to 13). 354 lines fewer. Measured against Q.6a: builds the same, formatting the corpus apps 0.55 s against 0.56 with 3% less CPU; the `syntax`, `format` and `outline` corpus suites are unchanged. Left out: SYN-03 and FMT-03, which want measuring; FMT-14, whose gain is in checking invariants and whose rewrite risks them; FMT-15, an API xtask's corpus code uses, for Q.13.

- [x] **Q.6c** `quality: follow-ups in the compiler`. The same for Q.6 and Q.6a: ordinary builds no longer recording the navigation index they discard (CF02); alias bodies canonicalized once (CF03); constructor dictionaries moved and patterns borrowed (CF04/05, CB14/21); annotation arity and payload types nothing reads (CF11, CB10/13); sequences built by front insertion or rebuilt suffixes (CF06/08/09, CB06/08/20/22); documentation types made only for what is documented (CB17/18); the recursive passes still without a stack guard (CF15, CB12). Measured against Q.6b.

  Done: an ordinary `canonicalize` runs with the recorder off and builds no navigation targets, which leaves no duplicates to remove (the editor's record-field lookup, which also discarded them, now calls it too); alias bodies are canonicalized once; imported unions are one shared `Arc<Union>` in interfaces, the canonical module, constructor patterns and the constructor table, which keeps a constructor's position rather than a copy of its arguments; operator chains are arranged with a stack, checked against the old arranger on every chain of up to six operators over eight fixities; annotation arity and payload types nothing read are gone; the solver and the interface readers share one converter; sequences are built forward; documentation types are made for what is documented, checked against the old writer on 3,000 random modules per release; and every recursive walk the audit listed is guarded, `iterated_dealias` now a loop. 53 lines fewer. Measured against Q.6b: docspell builds cold in 0.86 s against 0.89 with 5% less CPU, exosphere in 1.17 s against 1.20; the `check`, `graphs`, `objects`, `javascript` and `docs` corpus suites are unchanged. Left out: CF16 and CF17, CB11's merged report arms, CB24, CB26's direct streaming and the deduplicated decision paths, which want measuring or would split upstream's messages; deep `Type` clones, comparisons and drops are still unguarded.

- [x] **QF.1** `fix: tests that need Node fail without it`. Some test-runner tests skip when Node is missing, which the test tiers forbid (TEST-05). **Check:** with Node off the path, those tests fail.

  Done: three tests returned early without Node unless `CI` was set, the elm-test transcripts, `--watch` and the runner fixtures; they call testkit's `require_node`, which fails with a message saying Node isn't on the PATH. Run with an empty PATH, all three fail.

- [x] **QF.2** `fix: language-server comparisons count every answer`. The parity comparison iterates the expected requests only, so answers nomi adds pass unseen (FND-16, XTK-09). **Check:** a harness test where an extra answer fails, and every ratchet still passing.

  Done: answers past the recorded ones fail the session's diagnostics outcome, so a listed case breaks rather than a new one appearing unlisted; `cargo xtask corpus lsp` stops on an answer the recording lacks and names the projects it can't check for want of a recording. Every language-server ratchet passes, and the corpus suite's 7,359 other listed answers too. Surprises: the suite's 80 answers for octicons-elm fail in a debug build at every commit since they were recorded, its references outlasting the deadline, and pass optimized; Q.13a takes that up.

- [x] **QF.3** `fix: transcript timeouts cover stdin`. A transcript writes all of stdin before its clock starts or its output is drained, so a child that doesn't read can hang it (FND-17; xtask's copies of the loop, XTK-14, go with Q.13). **Check:** a child that never reads a large stdin ends at the timeout.

  Done: testkit's transcripts feed the case's input on a thread of its own, so the output readers and the time limit start as the command does; a test gives `sh -c "sleep 30"` a megabyte it never reads, and the run ends at its 200 ms limit, where the write used to block for the whole 30 seconds.

- [x] **QF.4** `fix: test workers read whole messages`. A worker parses each socket read as one JSON message; a summary split across reads crashes it. elm-test's own worker does the same, but nomi's supervisor can frame its one message, so the worker can buffer to a newline (TEST-01). **Check:** the audit's split-write reproduction passes, and the elm-test transcripts are unchanged.

  Done: the supervisor ends its summary with a newline, and both workers, nomi's `driver.js` and the vendored `after.js` (its patch noted in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`), buffer the socket and parse whole lines. `tests/worker_messages.rs` sends a summary in two writes 50 ms apart: the worker gets it whole, where it used to fail on half a JSON value. The elm-test transcripts and the 14 corpus suites are unchanged.

- [x] **QF.5** `fix: the cache codec rejects overflowing lengths`. A tenth LEB128 byte above 1 is truncated rather than rejected, so a corrupt artifact can decode (CF01, CB16). **Check:** unit tests over the tenth byte, and every valid encoding unchanged.

  Done: a tenth byte above 1 makes the length `None`, as a malformed input should be. A unit test walks every tenth byte from 2 to 0x7f besides the largest valid length; the interface test now starts with the current version and puts a huge length, then an overflowing one, where the home's length goes, and the second used to decode the rest of the interface under an empty home. Both tests fail on the old decoder. Valid encodings are unchanged.

- [x] **QF.6** `fix: language-server request identity and installs`. A reused request id can overwrite the context of one still pending, and installing a package through another VFS leaves the index and package suggestions stale (LSP-05/06); both verified first. **Check:** a test for overlapping requests with one id, and one for a request right after an install.

  Done: every request the server sends goes out under an id from one counter (`Server::ask`), and what it waits on is keyed by that id: an install question per question, the settings pull by its newest id, so an older answer no longer wins. After an install that wrote `elm.json`, the server marks its own VFS changed, drops its package list and schedules the check an edit would. `crates/cli/tests/nomi_lsp.rs` drives the `nomi lsp` binary offline, as an install needs its own cache and proxy settings, which only a child process can be given: two projects asking to install one package, answered in reverse, each act on their own; and a hover right after an install sees the package. Both failed on the old code.

- [x] **Q.7** `quality: codegen-js`. Alias expansion, cores, name order and the region shift from the compiler and base; statements and kernels written into the output buffer rather than built and copied, and the AST states and options nothing constructs (CODEGEN-01/03–07); the rest of its findings.

  Done: the printer, statements, kernels and main exports write straight into their output buffers, the kernel buffer starting with the program's header; calls are generated directly rather than built from cloned subtrees; names are made in one allocation; the reachability walk borrows the graph's globals; constructs nothing builds are gone; the debugger metadata's found aliases and unions are structs and its extraction and encoding apart; and the crate takes the compiler's alias expansion and `Home` helpers and base's name order, region shift and `char_width`. Generated JavaScript is byte-identical: the ratchets, the kernels test, the `javascript` corpus suite's 81 apps and a throwaway comparison of the old and new printers on 300,000 random expressions. Measured against QF.6 with code generated: warm builds take 110 ms against 150 on docspell and 100 against 120 on exosphere, cold builds the same or less. Left out: `strictEq` and `strictNEq` stay two functions, as their rows aren't each other's negation.

- [x] **Q.8** `quality: engine build`. The build itself: `graph`, `names`, `check`, `objects`, `make`, `schedule`, `artifacts`, `index`, `live`, `vfs`, `javascript`, `report` and their tests. `Walk::module` (211 lines, six mutex side channels) returning its results through `schedule::Outcome`; one `Build` request and result in place of six forwarders and three tuples; `make::build` (224 lines) split; dead code (`canonicalize_graph`); five ratchet test bodies as one. From the audit: the scheduler given the build's own snapshot, so a new revision cancels the work still queued for an old one, rather than a fresh snapshot that never goes stale (ENG-01); incremental indexing by a lookup built once rather than a search per module, the test environment built once, early errors rendered once, results returned rather than sent through side channels, module names sorted without strings (ENG-02–05/08/09). Measured against Q.7.

  Done: a module's job returns `Result<Made, Vec<Problem>>` through `schedule::Outcome`, where six mutexes and a re-sort carried its results, and splits into `cached`, `compile`, `checks` and `document`; one `check::Build { .. }.run(Goal)` replaces six forwarders and their tuples; `make::build` is `build`, `load`, `shown` and `write`, with one failure path and no out-parameter; the scheduler takes the build's own snapshot, so a language-server check for an old revision is cancelled rather than run (`a_new_revision_cancels_the_check`), and a cancelled save hands its flag to the next check; incremental indexing looks modules up rather than searching (`tests/index.rs`), the editor's environment and early errors are built once, module names sort as names; `canonicalize_graph` is gone; each ratchet test is one call into a shared body, and the test support is modules rather than `allow(dead_code)`. Sources grew by 108 lines (named structs and split functions), the tests shrank by 125. Measured against Q.7: the same, cold and warm, with code generated; the `check`, `graphs`, `objects`, `javascript` and `docs` corpus suites are unchanged. Left out: M4, upstream's own pre-build check in one expression, and M6, as creating parent directories would let `--docs` write where it fails today.

The ask-then-write match of install, update, remove and bump once; a `Captured` console and a command `Context`; `diff::Binop.associativity` as the compiler's `Associativity`; dead `Personality::program`; documentation entries borrowed in `diff`, and types compared without building syntax (ENG-06/07); the copies of `Name::parse` and `elm.json` paths project now offers once.

- [x] **QF.7** `fix: the artifact cache trims itself`. §6 promises a cache that trims itself, like Go's: `Artifacts::trim` removes what went unused for five days, at most once a day, but nothing calls it, so the cache only grows. A finished build or check runs it, its errors ignored as a cache write's are. **Check:** an entry unused past the limit is gone after a build, a recent one stays, and a second build within the day reads no directory.

  Done: `make::run` and `make::interfaces` (and so `check`, `make` and `nomi test`'s builds) trim the cache as they end, failed or not, and the language server trims it once as it starts, on a thread of its own, as an editor may be all that builds. `crates/cli/tests/cache_trim.rs` plants entries six and four days unused: after a check the first is gone and the second stays, and a third planted afterwards survives the next check within the day. Without the trim it fails.

An install from a code action runs once without input to learn its question, and the question the editor shows ends with `elm: <stdin>: hGetLine: end of file`, which that run writes after the prompt; after "Yes", the second run's message still begins with the first run's text. **Check:** the question shows the prompt alone, and the message after an answer only what the answered run says.

It fails on the old code.

- [x] **QF.9** `fix: names, not the comments before them`. A module name, variable pattern or literal can begin with a comment the parser placed inside the node (`module {- c -} Main`); hover, completion and xtask's error recorder took the node's first token, the comment, for the name. The first significant token is the name. **Check:** hover and completion on a module name preceded by a comment answer for the name, as elm-language-server's tree, which keeps comments out of the name, does.

  Done: a comment on a line of its own before a module name, variable pattern or literal belongs to that node in nomi's tree, so its first token was the comment. Hover's and completion's module names, and xtask's mutation generator's parameters, locals and literals, take the first significant token. A unit test hovers a module name below such a comment; it fails on the old code. The transcripts and the `lsp` corpus suite are unchanged.

Skipped: folding `deps` into `solve`, which would make `deps` need fetched projects and stop at an unreadable manifest. 177 lines more, the enums, the scanner and the new test. Every recorder that runs offline writes the same bytes as before, and the `check`, `graphs`, `objects`, `javascript`, `docs` and `names` suites give the unmodified xtask's results; CI and the other suites are unchanged. The runtime's exit findings were all QF.10's.

- [x] **QF.10** `fix: the runtime's tasks are checked`. A nested `null` or `undefined` task (`andThen(undefined, f)`, `uninterruptible(null)`, `scoped(undefined)`) makes the interpreter throw out of its drain, leaving the runtime awake and running no fiber again; and the `INTERRUPT` task tag and the `INTERRUPTED` exit kind are one string, so an interrupt exit passes for a task. With the runtime's five other exit findings. **Check:** a test for each, failing on the old runtime.

  Done: a nested task the interpreter enters (`andThen`, `onError`, the masks, `scoped`) is checked where it enters, one nullish test rather than one per step, so a missing one is that fiber's defect and the drain goes on; the interrupt task's tag is `interruptFiber`, apart from every exit kind; `main` turns an exit into a code and message with a pure `outcome` over the exported `ExitKind`, throwing on a kind it doesn't know where it exited 0; `main`'s unused `log` option, the exports nothing imports and the `fibers` map nothing read are gone, and every export has a doc comment. The model's random programs gain a missing-task kind, and four unit tests cover the rest; five of the 50 tests fail on the old runtime. The model properties pass across 200,000 seeds, and the chain, nested, error, scope, mask and ensure paths take the same time.


- [x] **QF.11** `fix: tied record fields and endless delays`. Types the compiler infers give every record field the same place, and upstream's `fieldsToList` breaks the tie in the release's name order; nomi's type printer broke it in byte order, so under 0.19.3 `main = { a = 1, bb = 2, c = 3 }` reported `{ a : number, bb : number2, c : number1 }` where elm 0.19.3 reports `{ a : number, c : number1, bb : number2 }`. And the runtime's test clock made a timer with a `NaN`, `undefined` or infinite delay due at that time, so virtual time stood at `NaN` or infinity for good. **Check:** a test for each, failing on the old code.

  Done: the type printer lists a record's fields through `fields_to_list`, as the optimizer and canonicalization do, so ties follow the release's name order in BAD MAIN TYPE and in docs; the test clock makes every delay that isn't a positive finite number due at once, as it did zero and negative ones. A unit test prints an inferred record under 0.19.1 and 0.19.3, and a clock test sets the four delays; both fail on the old code. CI is green.

- [x] **QF.12** `fix: links to nothing under a formatted directory`. elm-format lists a directory's entries as files unless they are directories, so a symbolic link to nothing is a file it passes over unless it is named `.elm`, which then fails when read; nomi's walk stopped at the link and reported the whole directory missing, so `--validate` on gingko, whose `client/app/README.md` links to nothing, exited 1 where elm-format exits 0. The scoreboard met it first at MQ's exit, gingko having joined the corpus after its last run. **Check:** a test, failing on the old code.

  Done: `vfs::discover` keeps a link to nothing as a file by its extension, as elm-format does, and goes on; one named `.elm` is read and reported as unreadable. A unit test walks a directory with both kinds, and gingko validates as elm-format does.

The second exit review (12 fresh reviews, kept in `~/.cache/nomi-dev/quality/exit2/`) found every first-review fix holding and the runtime clean, and 59 smaller places elsewhere: the halves of earlier fixes left undone (aliases still copied, `diff_alias`'s `unreachable!`, the formatter's invariants formatting each module again), helpers re-spelled beside their shared versions, allocations made only to be read, a few tuples and wildcard arms. The steps below fix them.

- [x] **Q.16a** `quality: second exit findings, syntax and format` (base, project and registry 2; syntax 6; format 6).

  Done: project's JSON parser reads through the outline scanner's `Scan` rather than a cursor of its own; elm's and elm-format's number scanners are one `chomp_number`, elm-format's copy gone; the parser's reports write the closing-bracket sentence and the type-name report once each, and the keywords one arm each; `\x` escapes fold through `int_digits`, and the AST generator writes its accessors straight into its output; the formatter's invariants take the output already made, so the fixtures and the corpus suite format each module once; literal bodies come from `literal_body`, links and docs names are written in place, and one `Block::lines` serves the three places that took a block's lines. 11 lines more, the keyword list and the shared scanner's two small enums, where elm-format's scanner lost about 70. Formatting the corpus apps takes 0.29 s, as before. CI and the `syntax`, `format` and `outline` corpus suites are unchanged.

- [x] **Q.16b** `quality: second exit findings, compiler, codegen-js, test and testkit` (compiler 10; codegen-js, test and testkit 7).

  Done: nomi's interfaces no longer store constructor indices and counts, which their places give (format version 3); one `env::one` builds the ambiguous-name error, one `dups::in_name_order` checks record fields, one `error::tuple` sizes tuples, and `Home::author_and_project` serves reports, interfaces and codegen; aliases are shared `Arc`s like unions, so module types copy neither; the error printer reads an alias's parts through a borrowed `AliasParts`, without its `unreachable!`; `children` pushes onto the solver's stack rather than building a list per node; the test runner parses each result once under `--report junit`; testkit's last wildcard over `Method`, its temporary strings and its too-wide items are gone. 41 lines more, the alias parts and the variant list. Builds and checks take the same time as Q.16a. CI and the `check`, `graphs`, `objects`, `javascript`, `docs`, `tests` and `lsp` corpus suites are unchanged.

- [x] **Q.16c** `quality: second exit findings, engine, ide, lsp and cli` (engine 5; ide 8; lsp and cli 5).

  Done: a package's build borrows its dependencies' interfaces rather than copying them, and returns its own alone; what a clean build made is one `Made`, docs or optimized code; kernel module names are project's `is_kernel` and `kernel_short`; the ide's outline, review input and hints are named, `ProjectOutline`, `Elm` and `hint_at`, one `tree_at` and one `covering_node` serve navigation and completion, and an imported value's kind decides its completion in one table; the refactorings' arguments are serde structs; the diff and publish tests serve the newest docs alone, as the registry only ever answers them, so the 18 older copies are gone; testkit's `transcript::matching` and `Case::lay_out_padded` serve the CLI, engine and xtask; the fake registry's methods are an enum. About 130 lines more, the named types. Builds take the same time as Q.16b. CI and every corpus suite are unchanged. Surprises: a package exposing a module only a dependency has no longer exports that dependency's interface, as upstream's `gatherInterfaces`; navigation into a package reads its files from the package cache, as completion did; the move refactorings read their whole parameters, a malformed one answering null as before.

- [x] **Q.16d** `quality: second exit findings, xtask` (10).

  Done: a run that hung is `Ran::Hung` rather than `None`; the names suite's chunks take a `Ready` struct; corpus case names are `App::case` and `Package::case`, where ten places spelled them; recorders and the engine's replay pin a case's `elm.json` through one testkit `Case::with_manifest`; the language-server corpus reads source directories through project's `Outline` and lists files with `files_below`, writes its sessions' steps as testkit's serialized `Step`s, and builds URIs and replacements through testkit's `dir_uri` and `dir_replacements`; the benchmark reads resident memory without a parameter that never varied; Helix logs' conversion to sessions is tested. 52 lines more, two tests most of it. The modules, javascript and optimized recorders write the same bytes, the language-server recorder the same but for the workspace-symbol order elm-language-server varies between runs, and `corpus lsp` generates the same 126 sessions. CI is green.

- [x] **Q.17** `quality: third exit findings` (27 across ten crate groups; the runtime clean).

  Done: kernel module names and authors are one `nomi_base::kernel` (`MODULE_PREFIX`, `short`, `is_module`, `is_author`), where project, codegen and engine each had theirs; `Compat::in_name_order` is base's, the type checker's copy gone; the default imports are the compiler's `foreign::default_import_names`, not a second engine list; the ide finds tests through engine's `paths::tests_dir` and `canonical_or_given`; the rest are the reviews' smaller places, names and needless copies. 34 lines more, base's kernel module most of it. One fix changes behavior: the index and the language server's live check list `.elm` files through `vfs::elm_files`, which passes over links to nothing as upstream does, where QF.12's walk kept them for the formatter alone; a test fails on the old code. Builds and formatting take the same time as Q.16c. CI and every corpus suite are unchanged. Not done, for a decision: `registry::solver::explain` and its `pubgrub` dependency have no caller outside a test, though DECISIONS.md (2026-10-08) and the architecture's §7 describe them as nomi's resolution errors; deleting them and correcting the documents is the recommendation.


The survey behind these steps (11 reviews, every file read: 118 major, 514 minor and 405 small findings, about 7,600 removable lines of 124,000) is kept in the development cache, `~/.cache/nomi-dev/quality/`, one report per crate group; a step without it re-reviews its crate first. Findings that would change behavior are not in this milestone: they are listed in its last step's commit for a decision.

**Exit:** a fresh review of each crate and the runtime against §13 finds nothing beyond taste; every ratchet, transcript and corpus suite is unchanged, but for what the QF fixes change on purpose; and the scoreboard loses no cell.

Done: the second review's 59 places are fixed (Q.16a–d) and its runtime review was clean; the third's 27 are fixed by Q.17, its runtime review clean too. Every corpus suite gives its numbers from before the milestone: syntax and format 1,949 of 1,949, check and graphs 5,739 projects, objects 5,733, javascript 81 apps, docs 5,652 packages, names 5,722 of 5,751, tests 14 of 14, hir 32,435 modules, and the language server's ratchet 7,439 of 8,182. The scoreboard's 385 rows, gingko's among them since QF.12, have nomi ahead on wall time, CPU time and memory in every cell. The behavior changes accepted along the way are listed in the exit commit for a decision.

## M11: Node platform and server runtime, release 0.5

This needs M8 and lane R. At the start of M11 we review package names and APIs together, after writing real server code against drafts.

The opening review takes [the drafts](drafts/m11-apis.md) and decides, besides the names: where `Platform` lives (draft: base, with a CI check that the compiler never names it, R1); one self-starting `.mjs` per entry against a module and a separate shim (draft: one file; `nomi build --platform node` without an entry writes a plain module exporting `Elm`); nomi/* versions (draft: 1.0.0, bumped together, apart from the toolchain's 0.x, since elm.json needs a package version); how the FFI check finds exports (draft: a lexical scan at build time, plus a startup check for names that aren't literals); and effectful tests (11.10a). elm-explorations/test's `Test` is pure, so the draft's `Nomi.Test.effect` can't hold a task as written; the draft answer is a nomi/test kernel marking the test, run by nomi's vendored runner on the Node runtime only when nomi/test is a test dependency.

Platform.js is not overridden, though R1 lists it: it differs across elm/core 1.0.0–1.0.2, 1.0.3–1.0.4 and 1.0.5, whose change fixed effect ordering, and it needs only the Scheduler API, which is byte-identical across 1.0.x; so the Scheduler is overridden, and the program lifecycle goes in the Nomi kernel and 11.3d (a `DECISIONS.md` entry with 11.3a). Kernels are baked into package artifacts under one namespace keyed by short name (`Global::kernel`), so an override replaces that node when the engine links, and artifacts stay platform-free; each override is keyed by the hash of the upstream file it replaces, so an unknown release fails loudly instead of linking a mismatched kernel.

- [x] **11.1a** `codegen-js: platforms and ES module output`. A `Platform` (`Browser`, `Node`) and an output format (`Iife`, `EsModule`), two enums: a Node program can still be an IIFE, which the test runner needs. `EsModule` wraps the same function as `const scope = {};` … `}(scope)); export const Elm = scope.Elm;`, since a module's `this` is undefined; `Iife` is today's bytes. The choice comes through `Make` and `javascript::program` to `generate`; `elm make` is unchanged. **Check:** every codegen ratchet and the javascript corpus suite are unchanged, and a `Platform.worker` fixture built as a module and imported by Node answers a message sent to an incoming port on an outgoing one.

  Done: codegen-js's `Target` holds the `Platform` and the `Format`, both defaulting to upstream's; `nomi build --output=x.mjs` writes the module, any other output today's bytes, and `elm make` still refuses `.mjs`. Nothing reads the platform yet; 11.1b gives it a flag. Tests: a port worker built as `.mjs` and imported by a Node driver answers `ping` with `PING`; the module is the IIFE's body, opened and closed differently. CI is green.
- [x] **11.1b** `codegen-js: entry shim`. A Node entry's module ends in a few generated lines that call `Elm.<Main>.init()` and leave room for 11.12's probe. **Check:** `node dist/x.mjs` runs a worker fixture whose `init` logs; the module without its shim runs nothing.

  Done: `nomi build --platform=node` with an `.mjs` output of one `main` ends in `Elm.<Main>.init();`, before which 11.12's probe goes; several mains, or the browser, give the plain module, and a `.js` output stays an IIFE. Tests: `node dist/entry.mjs` runs a worker whose `init` logs, and the same project built as a plain module prints nothing; a Node module of two mains is the browser's. CI is green, and the javascript corpus suite gives its 81 apps after 11.1a and 11.1b.
- [x] **11.2** `codegen-js: platform availability check`. Runs on what the program reaches and names the import path in the error (R1). Generation already writes only what each `main` reaches; a pure breadth-first `reach` with parent links shares one `deps_of(node)` with generation, so the two can't drift. A table per platform lists what is unavailable: Node lacks `Browser`, `VirtualDom` and `File`; the browser lacks every nomi/* kernel. The error shows the whole chain, `main → Page.view → Html.div → VirtualDom.node → Elm.Kernel.VirtualDom`. **Check:** a project importing a module with an unused `view` builds for Node and fails, with the exact path text, once `main` calls it; building one project for both platforms writes no new artifact.

  Done: codegen-js's `reach` walks what the `main`s reach breadth first, keeping each global's parent, over the `deps_of` generation now follows too. Node lacks `Browser`, `VirtualDom` and `File`, and a build reaching one fails `NOT AVAILABLE ON NODE` with the nearest such kernel's chain, `main → Page.view → Html.div → Elm.Kernel.VirtualDom` (elm/html's `div` calls the kernel itself). The graph names kernels by short name alone, keeping no author, so the browser's table is a list of short names, empty until 11.4 adds nomi/*'s; an empty table walks nothing. Tests: CLI transcripts for an unused `view`, which builds, and a used one, which fails with its chain; a browser build then a Node build leave the cache and project byte for byte; `reach`'s chains and the debugger kernel outside `--debug`. Generation writes the same bytes (javascript corpus suite: 81 apps) in the same time. CI is green.
- [x] **11.3a** `runtime: the runtime in the program, and Scheduler on it`. runtime/src is embedded as test's runner is and prefixed to Node programs as one `_Runtime` namespace, through a pure transform of its `import` and `export` lines (main.js stops importing `node:util`). Node kernel overrides live in `runtime/node/kernel/<author>/<project>/<Module>.js`. The Scheduler override keeps every `_Scheduler_*` name other kernels use on the lane-R runtime: `binding` is `async`, `rawSpawn` a daemon, as `Process.spawn` outlives its spawner, `kill` doesn't wait, as upstream's doesn't; the runtime gains per-fiber mailboxes for `receive` and `rawSend`, and drains synchronously when called outside a drain, as upstream's `_Scheduler_working` does, so ports answer in the same tick. **Check:** the runtime's properties pass at 10,000 seeds, and elm-test's runner built for Node with these kernels gives every recorded transcript through every reporter: corpus `Platform.worker` programs run unchanged.

  Done: codegen-js embeds runtime/src and puts it ahead of a Node program's kernels as `_Runtime`, through a pure transform of its `import` and `export` lines; main.js no longer imports `node:util`. The engine replaces a kernel when it links, keyed by the SHA-256 of the upstream file, and a release it doesn't know fails as not available on Node, naming the package's version; no cache key changed. Node's Scheduler keeps every `_Scheduler_*` name on one runtime, `synchronous`, which drains at once when work arrives outside a drain; fibers have mailboxes, and `interruptFork` interrupts without waiting. `nomi test` builds its runner for Node, with the DOM's kernels its stand-ins allow (`Dom::Polyfilled`; two `DECISIONS.md` entries, this and Platform.js). One piece of 11.3b came with it: an outgoing port sleeps 0 ms after each message, which upstream's scheduler shortens by a quirk, so Node's `Process.sleep` runs on the runtime's clock, a zero delay on `setImmediate`, and the corpus suites take the time they took. Tests: the runtime's properties at 10,000 seeds, with mailboxes and the synchronous mode; every elm-test transcript and `corpus tests`' 14 suites; a port worker answers before `send` returns, for the browser and Node; an unknown release of a replaced kernel fails. Browser output is unchanged (javascript corpus suite: 81 apps). One difference from upstream, on Node only: a JS exception inside a process is the fiber's defect, reported, rather than thrown to the caller. CI is green.
- [x] **11.3b** `runtime: Process and Time on the runtime clock`. Overrides elm/core's `Process.js` and elm/time's `Time.js`: `sleep`, `now` and `setInterval` (a daemon fiber that sleeps and spawns) go through the program's clock, chosen at startup; the time zone functions stay upstream's. **Check:** an Elm fixture sleeping an hour, taking three `Time.every 1000` ticks and reading `Time.now` runs under the virtual clock in under a second, and prints the same sequence under the real clock.

  Done: `Process.sleep` came with 11.3a. elm/time 1.0.0's `Time.js`, one file in every copy, is replaced: `now` reads the runtime's clock, and an interval is a loop that sleeps and starts its task on a daemon (it drifts as timers do; no catch-up); the zone functions are upstream's. The clock is chosen when the Scheduler's runtime is made: the virtual one under `NOMI_CLOCK=virtual`, which runtime/README.md documents, until 11.10b chooses it its own way. Test: a worker that sleeps an hour, takes three `Time.every 1000` ticks and reads `Time.now` exits in under a second on the virtual clock, and prints the same lines on the real clock with its waits in milliseconds. `corpus tests` gives its 14 suites. CI is green.
- [x] **11.3c** `runtime: Http via fetch`. elm/http 2.0.0's kernel on `fetch`, cancelled through `AbortController`; 1.0.0 gets 11.3a's unknown-release error. `fetch` has no upload progress, so `Http.track` reports only `Receiving` (a `DECISIONS.md` entry). **Check:** against a loopback server, GET and POST, `expectJson`, a bad status, a timeout, `Http.cancel` and a bad URL each give the `Result` the browser kernel gives.

  Done: elm/http 2.0.0's `Http.js` is replaced on Node by one on `fetch` that keeps every name `Http.elm` calls; a request `Request` refuses is `BadUrl_`, a rejected fetch or body read `NetworkError_`, the timeout covers the whole response and aborts it, and a killed process aborts its request and answers nothing, as `xhr.abort()` does. Headers come as XHR gives them, lower case, repeats joined, `Set-Cookie` left out; a GET sends no body. `Http.track` reports `Receiving` alone (`DECISIONS.md`). On Node there is no document, so a relative URL is `BadUrl`, and text is read as UTF-8 whatever its charset. The timeout is the host's, real under the virtual clock as the network is. Tests: against a loopback server, GET, response metadata, POST with `expectJson`, bytes, `BadStatus 404`, `Timeout`, `NetworkError`, `BadUrl`, and a tracked request cancelled after its first progress, whose connection closes; a changed `Http.js` in the cache fails as an unknown release, as 1.0.0's does. CI is green.
- [x] **11.3d** `runtime: worker lifecycle on Node`. A `Platform.worker` entry exits 130 or 143 on SIGINT or SIGTERM after the runtime shuts down, and stays alive while a subscription or timer is live. **Check:** SIGTERM to a `Time.every` worker gives 143 within the deadline.

  Done: main.js's signal, deadline and shutdown code is one `lifecycle`, which `main` and a new `worker(runtime)` share: a worker's first signal shuts its runtime down, interrupting fibers and running finalizers, and exits 130 or 143; a second signal or the deadline exits at once; until then the process lives as long as Node's event loop has work. The shim moved inside the module's function, where the program's runtime is, and starts the lifecycle before `init`, so no signal meets Node's default handler; 11.4b chooses between `main` and `worker` per entry kind. Tests: a `Time.every` worker built as an entry ticks until SIGTERM gives 143 and SIGINT 130, run on Node's own binary (a version manager's wrapper exits 143 itself); in the runtime, a ticking worker's finalizer runs on either signal, and one with nothing live exits 0. `corpus tests` gives its 14 suites. CI is green.
- [x] **11.4a** `registry: bundled nomi/* packages`. `packages/` is embedded and extracted once per content hash into nomi's cache; an overlay on the solver's `Packages` lists each nomi/* package at its bundled version alone, the store serves its directory, and nomi/* never reaches the index or the network. Kernel trust (`nomi_base::kernel`) accepts `nomi`, safe because nothing named nomi/* can come from the registry. **Check:** offline with an empty store, an app depending on a fixture nomi/* package builds and calls its kernel; a fixture index listing a newer release is never read; `nomi add` writes elm.json.

  Done: registry's `bundle` embeds `packages/` and extracts it once per hash under the cache's `bundled/`, or reads `NOMI_BUNDLE`'s directory, the test seam beside `NOMI_REGISTRY`; the list of files is empty until 11.4b adds nomi/core's. The index drops every nomi/* release but counts it, so `since/N` stays right (the registry has none today); a store carrying the bundle answers nomi/* from it alone, and a version it lacks is `NOT BUNDLED`. Only nomi's personality has that store: `elm install nomi/x` is an unknown package as before. Kernel trust accepts `nomi`, so a local package project named nomi/* is now parsed as a kernel source under `elm make` too. The browser's unavailable kernels are `BUNDLED_KERNELS`, empty, with a test that it lists the bundle's kernel files. `nomi test` builds as elm does, so nomi/* test dependencies wait for 11.10. Tests: offline with an empty store and an index listing a newer release, an app on a fixture nomi/fixture builds for Node and its kernel prints; `nomi add` writes `"nomi/fixture": "1.0.0"` and asks the registry nothing of it; `elm install` doesn't know it. CI is green.
- [x] **11.4b** `packages: nomi/core program and Env`, as reviewed. `Nomi.program`'s kernel starts the root fiber through the runtime's `main`; the platform defaults to Node when `main`'s annotation is nomi/core's program alias. **Check:** Elm fixtures: success exits 0; a failure prints to stderr and exits 1; SIGTERM runs a finalizer and exits 143; arguments arrive; a browser build of the same program fails with 11.2's path.

  Done: `packages/core` is nomi/core 1.0.0, bundled: `Nomi.Program`, `Nomi.program` and an `Env` of `args` and `vars` (11.7 adds the capabilities), and `Nomi.Task.ensure`, which 11.5a's module grows from. The Nomi kernel's `init` runs the task under the runtime's `main` on the program's runtime and marks itself, so the shim starts a worker's lifecycle only for an `init` without the mark: one lifecycle per entry, whatever `main`'s annotation says. Without `--platform`, nomi builds for Node when every exposed root `main` is the `Nomi.Program` alias in the root's interface. Bundled kernels' short names start with `Nomi`, which no elm/* kernel's does; the browser lacks them. `Nomi.log` waits for the review, as the draft says. Tests, from the real bundle offline: arguments and a variable arrive and success exits 0; `Task.fail "x"` prints `nomi: the program failed: x` and exits 1; SIGTERM runs an `ensure` finalizer and exits 143; a browser build fails with `main → Nomi.Task.ensure → Elm.Kernel.Nomi`. The tests and javascript corpus suites are unchanged. CI is green.
- [x] **11.5a** `packages: Fiber and the concurrent Task combinators`, as reviewed. **Check:** Elm fixtures under the virtual clock: `both`'s first failure interrupts the other and its finalizer runs; `race`'s loser is interrupted; `timeout` gives `Nothing` and runs the finalizer; an `uninterruptible` region interrupted midway runs to its end; joining a defect is the joiner's defect.

  Done: `Nomi.Task` has `both`, `all`, `race`, `timeout`, `acquireRelease`, `ensure`, `uninterruptible`, `fromResult` and `fromMaybe`, and `Nomi.Fiber` the opaque `Fiber`, `fork`, `forkDaemon`, `join`, `await`, `interrupt` and `Exit`, all thin over the runtime in the Nomi kernel. The runtime gains `all` and `race` over one `gather`, which forks into its own scope and watches exits as a wait, so the virtual clock advances and a watched defect is passed on, not reported too; `race` ends as the first task ends, failure included, as the draft says. `timeout` sleeps on the program's clock; `acquireRelease` is scoped to its use function; a `Defect` carries the JS error's text. Fixed on the way: under the synchronous runtime a program's root fiber could end before `main` watched it, so a defect printed twice; `Runtime.fork` takes an observer attached before the fiber starts. Tests: one program under the virtual clock prints each combinator's ending, interruption and finalizers (`both`, `all`'s order, `race`, `timeout`, an interrupted `uninterruptible` region run to its end, the four `Exit`s, a daemon outliving its parent, `acquireRelease` on success, failure and interruption); a `Debug.todo` in a joined fiber exits 1 with one report; runtime tests for `all` and `race`. `corpus tests` gives its 14 suites. CI is green.
- [x] **11.5b** `packages: Ref and Queue`, as reviewed. **Check:** 100 fibers each `modify` a counter once and it reads 100; a bounded queue's `offer` waits while full, in order; `poll` on an empty queue gives `Nothing`.

  Done: `Nomi.Ref` is a cell whose every operation is one runtime step, so `modify` reads and writes with nothing between; `Nomi.Queue` keeps its items and two FIFO lines of waiting takers and offerers, an offer going straight to the oldest taker. Waiting is the runtime's new `wait`, a suspension on another fiber rather than the host, so the virtual clock runs on; an interrupted waiter leaves its line, and what a waker hands over runs before an interruption that follows it, so no item is lost. `bounded` of 0 or less has no room: each offer waits for a take. Tests: 100 fibers' `modify`s read 100; a `bounded 2` queue's third offer waits and items come out in order; `poll` of an empty queue is `Nothing`; takers are served in order; an interrupted `take` loses no item and an interrupted `offer` adds none; runtime tests for `wait`. A million chained steps take the time they took; a suspended fiber is 1,290 bytes, 50 more than before 11.3a's mailboxes, which P.17's lazy fields take back. CI is green.
- [x] **11.6a** `runtime: draining`. The lane-R lifecycle interrupts the root at once and scope closure interrupts children first, which would cut in-flight requests off; so a drain phase: on the first signal the server stops accepting, in-flight request fibers run until they finish or the deadline passes, then the rest are interrupted and finalizers run. Request fibers belong to the server's scope, not the handler's caller. **Check:** a test for each of the three phases.

  Done: the runtime's `draining(ms, use)` runs `use(spawn)` in a scope that drains however it ends: `spawn` forks into that scope from anywhere inside it, so request fibers are the server's; on closing, `spawn` starts nothing (its fiber is interrupted before it runs), the scope waits for its fibers or `ms` on the runtime's clock, whichever is first, then interrupts the rest and runs its finalizers. The drain is masked like any closing, so main.js's real-time deadline still bounds the whole shutdown and a second signal still exits: the drain's deadline is the softer one inside it. The step loop is untouched; a closing flag lives on a subclass, so plain scopes are no larger. Tests on the virtual clock: a closed scope starts nothing; in-flight fibers that end in time keep their results and the drain ends with them, leaving no timer; past the deadline the rest are interrupted and finalized before the scope's own finalizers; a scope that ends by itself drains too. CI is green.

  Done: `packages/server` is nomi/server 1.0.0, bundled with the draft's `Nomi.Server`; `defaults` drains for 5 seconds, and `listen` lengthens the lifecycle's real-time deadline to the drain and 5 more through main.js's `allowShutdown`, so the drain's finalizers run. `Net` is an opaque token in `Nomi`, an `Env` field. The `NomiServer` kernel loads `node:http` with `process.getBuiltinModule` (Node 22.3, for 11.12's probe) and serves under 11.6a's `draining`: interrupting `listen` stops accepting, drains, then closes the remaining connections. Bodies are read once, `TooLarge` past 1 MiB, declared or chunked, and the rest drained so the client hears the answer; a client gone mid-body is `Disconnected`. `withHeader` replaces a header whatever its case; `listen` unwraps responses before the kernel sees them, so `--optimize` renames nothing it reads. Tests, a loopback client against a built server: eight methods, a percent-decoded path, a query and a header read case-blind, then SIGTERM's 143; a JSON body, `BadJson`, `TooLarge` declared and chunked, and exactly 1 MiB accepted; a taken port fails with `the address is already in use`; the runtime's lengthened deadline. The draft's example compiles with its file and stdio parts stubbed. CI is green.

A client disconnect interrupts its request; a defect answers 500 with a stderr line; SIGTERM drains through 11.6a. **Check:** a disconnect runs the handler's finalizer; `Debug.todo` in a handler gives 500 and one line; after SIGTERM an in-flight response completes, a new connection is refused and the process exits within the deadline.

A response closed before it ended interrupts its fiber, or the fiber as it starts if the client left first, so the handler's finalizers run. A defect answers `500 internal server error`, or destroys the socket if headers went out, and writes one line, `nomi: request GET /todo?x=1 died: <the defect on one line>`. A request still running when the drain ends has its connection closed, by 11.6b's last step. One fiber more per request, which P.17 may fold away. Tests, a loopback client against a built server: a dropped connection runs the handler's `ensure` and the server serves on; `Debug.todo` gives 500 and one stderr line, then a 200; after SIGTERM new connections are refused while a held-back request completes, and the process exits 143; past a one-second drain the request's connection closes, its finalizer runs and the process exits 143 without the forced deadline. What the scan can't confirm is checked when the module starts, right after its import: each literal name it couldn't see must be a function; a name made at run time is checked when called, `Threw` if it names no exported function (the check that every export be a function went in 11.12b).
