## MB: Backend platform, release 0.7

Goal: backend Elm done the Elm way. Elm has no FFI: every effect comes from the platform's own packages, with typed APIs, and packages are pure Elm. M11 shipped `Nomi.Ffi` instead of designing what a backend needs, so MB removes it and builds the platform it stood in for: the low-level blocks an ecosystem grows on in pure Elm (bytes and streams, processes, sockets, files), and, as Bun does, batteries a backend reaches for every day (crypto, SQL and key-value drivers, object storage). Bun becomes the only host (MB.4), so the kernels adapt Bun's built-ins rather than write protocols, never npm.

MB comes before MP, so that MP measures the platform as it will stay. MB.1 removes the FFI; MB.2 is the design, agreed with the maintainer; MB.3 onward build it, each module with its fake, its interruption guarantees and their tests. Postgres, MySQL, Valkey and an S3 server run for real in containers pinned by digest, a tier-2 suite (MB.15). Where an OS lacks what a module uses (signals and Unix sockets on Windows), the module says what it does there, and its check says on which OS each guarantee holds.

- [x] **MB.1** `platform: remove the FFI`. `Nomi.Ffi`, its kernel, codegen's export scan and import, `nomi build --ffi`, nomi.toml's `ffi`, the FFI reports, watch and fuzz targets, tests and docs go; DECISIONS.md says why. **Check:** CI green; nothing in the tree names the FFI but this plan's history and DECISIONS; nomi.toml with `ffi` is an unknown key at its span.

  Done: `Nomi.Ffi` and its `NomiFfi` kernel, codegen-js's export scan and `Ffi` import (`Format::EsModule` is now a unit variant), `nomi build --ffi`, nomi.toml's `ffi` key, the reports NO FFI MODULE, UNKNOWN FFI FUNCTION, FFI NEEDS NODE, FFI NEEDS AN ES MODULE and FFI MODULE NOT FOUND, the watch of FFI modules, the `ffi` fuzz target and its seeds, and `nomi_ffi.rs` are gone; nomi/core no longer depends on elm/json, which only `Nomi.Ffi` used. DECISIONS.md says why, and architecture §8 says nomi has no FFI. An `ffi` key in nomi.toml is UNKNOWN KEY at its span. The `nomi run` tests that used FFI calls as gates use the platform instead: a program polls `Fs.exists` for its go file, a slow shutdown is a server's minute-long drain with a finalizer sleeping that minute, and the helper holding a program's pipes is started by a `node` wrapper on the PATH. Tests: the manifest table's `ffi` case, the rewritten run tests, two tree transcripts without elm/json under nomi/core. CI is green.
- [x] **MB.2** `design: the backend platform`. A draft in `docs/drafts/mb-apis.md`: the bytes and streams model every module shares; capabilities, which `Env` carries and which stay ambient; how `nomi/test` fakes each; the modules in tiers, from processes, files, sockets and an HTTP server with streaming bodies and WebSockets to crypto, encodings, compression, SQLite, Postgres, Redis and S3, each with its Elm API and what Node gives to build it on; package boundaries and versions. **Check:** the maintainer agrees the draft; its decisions move into architecture.md §8 and the steps after this one are written from it.

  Done: `docs/drafts/mb-apis.md` holds the agreed platform: no FFI, Bun as the only host, bytes as the unit of I/O, pull streams with backpressure, capabilities in `Env` with a fake each, scoped resources, typed errors, the packages (`nomi/core`, `nomi/net`, `nomi/http`, `nomi/sql`, `nomi/redis`, `nomi/s3`, `nomi/test`), one SQL module over `Bun.sql`, and the order of work. It rests on a survey of Bun, Deno, Go, Gleam, Node, Effect, ZIO and Roc, and on a spike that ran the suites on Bun 1.4.3 and checked its built-ins against the guarantees: everything held but `Bun.sql`, which cannot cancel a running query, so the kernel cancels it from another connection. Architecture §8 and DECISIONS.md carry the decisions; MB.3 to MB.18 are written from §6. The MB steps were numbered B.n, which M8b already used, and are now MB.n.

- [x] **MB.3** `runtime: the same on any host`. Still on Node, so the switch to Bun changes only the host: the runtime formats a defect itself (its name, message and stack), so a report reads the same whatever the host prints, and a test worker exits when its supervisor's socket closes rather than relying on the host to notice. **Check:** a defect's report matches one snapshot; a worker whose supervisor is SIGKILLed exits; every suite unchanged.

  Done: the runtime formats a defect and a program's failure itself (`describeDefect` in fiber.js: name and message, then only the stack's frames), so nothing reaches `console.error` as an object for the host to print its own way; the defect report is checked line for line, and the runtime's tests now pass on Bun too (88 of 88). The server checks a port's range itself, so a port out of range fails with nomi's message, not Node's. A test worker (nomi's driver and `after.js`) exits when its supervisor's socket closes: `a_worker_exits_when_its_supervisor_is_killed` SIGKILLs `nomi test` while a test holds a listening server and finds no worker left, where before one was orphaned. Three build transcripts record the runtime's new size.
- [x] **MB.4** `platform: Bun as the host`. `nomi run`, `nomi run --watch`, `nomi test` and the testkit start `bun`, a built entry targets Bun, and the runtime's probe refuses another host or Bun before 1.4 with a message. nomi.toml's `platform = "node"` and `--platform=node` become `"bun"`, and `"node"` is UNKNOWN PLATFORM naming it (DECISIONS). `nomi test` runs every suite on Bun, a browser project's elm-test suite included, and says where to get Bun when it's missing. The server kernel moves to `Bun.serve`, keeping today's API until MB.9, and the server in a test no longer hangs; the leak probe reads Bun's handles. references.toml's `node-minimum` becomes `bun-minimum`, CI installs Bun by a pinned action, and README, architecture §8–9 and this plan's test tiers say Bun. **Check:** every suite that ran on Node runs on Bun with no Node on the PATH, but tier 3's oracles, which run upstream's Node tools; the tests corpus suite unchanged on Bun (14 of 14); a program run under `node` is refused with the probe's message; `platform = "node"` is reported at its span.

  Done: every suite runs on Bun 1.4 with Node hidden from the PATH (a `node` that fails loudly), 917 tests, and the tests corpus suite gives 14 of 14 as elm-test did. The probe refuses Node and an older Bun with one message, tested by running a built program in `node:vm` with a fake host, since Bun won't let `Bun.version` be faked; `platform = "node"` is UNKNOWN PLATFORM at its span. The server kernel is on `Bun.serve`, so HTTP follows Fetch's rules (DECISIONS 2026-10-19): repeated headers join, a status Fetch refuses answers 500, `respond` lists headers in lower case and in order, Bun's 10-second limit on a request in flight is off, and the drain closes leftover connections a macrotask after the last responses so Bun writes them first. Bun's `fetch` honours proxy variables, which hung the server-in-a-test case under the tests' blackhole proxy; the tests now exempt loopback. The leak probe counts fibers from Bun's heap snapshot and handles from `/dev/fd`. `node-minimum` stays in references.toml for the tier-3 oracles alone; the elm-review stand-in in the LSP tests runs on Bun. CI installs Bun 1.4.3 through `oven-sh/setup-bun` pinned by commit.
- [x] **MB.5** `core: Nomi.Bytes`. **Check:** each function against elm/bytes' encoders and decoders on generated input; strict UTF-8 refuses each class of bad sequence; base64 and hex round-trip.

  Done: `Nomi.Bytes` in nomi/core, a thin kernel on Bun's built-ins: `Uint8Array`'s base64 and hex (strict on the last group's unused bits, and refusing whitespace, which the built-in skips), a fatal `TextDecoder` that keeps a byte order mark, and `Buffer.indexOf` for searching. Slices share their buffer, as elm/bytes' `Decode.bytes` does. `slice` takes indexes as `String.slice` does, `fromBase64` reads either alphabet padded or not, and the docs say `==` can't tell `Bytes` apart. The elm-test suite `crates/cli/tests/nomi_bytes/` runs 44 tests, fuzzed 500 times: every function against elm/bytes' encoders and decoders, twelve classes of bad UTF-8 refused alone and inside text, the edges of each UTF-8 length read, RFC 4648's vectors, and round trips. Breaking slice, strict decoding and strict base64 in the kernel fails 18 of them.
- [x] **MB.6** `core: streams`. `Nomi.Stream` (D3): its `Error`, `Stream.reader` and `Stream.writer` built from Elm tasks, and transforms and `lines` made by tasks, since each holds state; `Stdio` on it, its text helpers decoding UTF-8 strictly; `isTerminal` and `size`; nomi/core and nomi/test 2.0.0 (D8), and a project still asking for 1.x told what nomi bundles. **Check:** a slow writer suspends its producer; an interrupted `read` loses nothing; lines split across chunks, and `line` in a loop on one `LineReader` loses no buffered text; a reader built from a task streams as a kernel one does, concurrent reads queued in order; invalid UTF-8 on stdin fails `Stdio.line` with `Invalid`; the fakes give the same results.

  Done: `Nomi.Stream` (D3) with `Reader`, `Writer`, `LineReader` and one `Error`; `readAll`, `limit` and `pipe` are Elm on the kernel's `read` and `write`. A reader's source only adds to its buffer, and a read takes from it only as the runtime hands it over, so an interrupted read loses nothing and reads, lines included, are answered in order; the runtime gained `waitHost`, `wait`'s hand-over for a host's data, which the test clock waits for. A reader made by `Stream.reader` runs its task on a fiber of the fiber that made it, only while a read waits; once that fiber ends, reads fail with `Closed`. Lines leave the rest of the stream in the reader, for the next line or read. `Stdio` is on it: `env.stdin` is a `Reader` and `stdout`/`stderr` are `Writer`s, `print` and `printLine` fail with `Stream.Error` (DECISIONS 2026-10-19), `line` fails with `Invalid` on bad UTF-8, and `isTerminal`, `isInteractive` (a reader's, beside the plan's two) and `size`. nomi/test 2.0.0 adds `withStdinBytes` and `withTerminal`. nomi/core and nomi/test are 2.0.0, and a project asking for a release nomi doesn't bundle gets PACKAGE NOT BUNDLED naming the one it does. The suite `nomi_suites/stream` (20 tests on the virtual clock) and nomi_capabilities (real stdin against its fake, invalid UTF-8 included; a line read interrupted while input is on its way; a pipe nobody reads holding back writes to stdout) check it; breaking demand or line pushback fails 7 of the suite's tests. A server's bundle grew 11 KB with the stream kernel. Noticed on the way: a program whose fibers all wait on each other exits 0 when the event loop empties.
- [x] **MB.7** `core: files and paths`. The rest of `Fs`, streaming files, `watch` as a handle (D6), `Nomi.Path`, `subdir` resolving symlinks; `Fs.memory` doing all of it. **Check:** each function on disk and in memory alike; a symlink out of a `subdir` is refused; a file opened in an interrupted task is closed, and a watch ends with its fiber; `Fs.read` of invalid UTF-8 fails.

  Done: `Nomi.Fs` adds `stat` (its time the program's clock in memory), `rename`, `copy` (recursive, links copied as links), `remove` with `{ recursive }` and `NotEmpty`, `symlink` (a target that leaves the capability or is absolute refused), `readLink`, `realPath` (from the capability's root, never beyond), `temporaryDirectory` (a capability the fiber owns, removed when it ends, only from one that may write), files as streams (`reader`, `writer`, `appender`, owned by the fiber that opens them, in place of D6's `withFile`: DECISIONS 2026-10-19), and `watch` with `change`, which gathers paths, each once, until taken, a thousand at most before saying `.`. `Fs.read` fails with `NotUtf8`. `Nomi.Path` is pure Elm (`join`, `normalize`, `parent`, `name`, `extension`, `relative` as a `Maybe`), but `matches`, on `Bun.Glob`. The kernel resolves paths with one algorithm for both stores: in memory a file system now has symbolic links, followed as the disk follows them. The shared table grew to 132 cases, each the same on disk and in memory; the links test refuses every new function through a link out of the root; a file opened by an interrupted fiber is closed (`/proc/<pid>/fd`), and a watch whose fiber ended answers a waiting `change` with `Nothing` (Bun keeps one inotify descriptor for all watches, so descriptors can't show it). The suite `nomi_suites/fs` (19 tests) checks watches, file times, streams and `Nomi.Path` with properties. nomi/core depends on elm/time now, so an application pins it; a server's DEV bundle grew 24 KB with the store.
- [x] **MB.8** `core: processes and signals`. `Command`, `output`, `spawn`, `kill`, the `processes` capability and `Process.fake`; `cwd`, `pid`, `platform`, `signals` as a handle (D6), with SIGINT and SIGTERM still ending the program. **Check:** an interrupted fiber's child gets SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after the grace, and on Windows ends at once; arguments reach the child unsplit; SIGHUP arrives through `signals` where the OS has it; a program's commands give the same results on `Process.fake` as on the system, and `Process.ran` lists them with what each was given on `stdin`; a fake child's `wait` returns once its `stdin` is closed.

  Done: `Nomi.Process` runs a `Command` (a program and its arguments as a list, `withCwd`, `withEnv`, `withStdin`, and `withGrace`, which the design left unsaid) through `env.processes`: `output` to its end in a scope of its own, `spawn` a `Child` with `stdin` a `Writer` and `stdout`/`stderr` `Reader`s, `wait`, `kill`; `cwd`, `pid`, `platform`; `signals` with `signal` giving a `Maybe` (D6). A child is the spawning fiber's: SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after its grace. `Process.fake` answers from a function, its children's input kept for `ran` and their `wait` returning once it is closed; nomi/test's `withCommands` makes one per run, and a test's default `Env` runs no command. The stream kernel gained readers of web streams and writers to Bun's `FileSink`, for MB.9 too. Checked: the same commands give the same results on the system and on a fake, whose `ran` lists them with their input; arguments reach `printf` unsplit; a child ignoring SIGTERM is killed after its 500 ms grace; SIGHUP arrives through `signals` while SIGTERM still exits 143; a fake child exits once its input closes; the suite `nomi_suites/process`. On the way, a program whose fibers all wait for each other no longer exits 0 silently: the runtime stops it, says it is stuck and exits 1, and a fiber waiting for a signal keeps the process alive (DECISIONS 2026-10-19). Kernels link whole, as upstream's do, so every program carries the process, file and stream kernels: a DEV hello-world server is 189 KB, worth splitting for MP.
- [ ] **MB.9** `http: a streaming server`. `nomi/server` becomes `nomi/http` 2.0.0, its server `Nomi.Http.Server`: request bodies as `Reader`s with limits; responses of text, JSON, bytes, a `Reader` or a file; SSE; TLS. A project still naming `nomi/server` is told its new name. **Check:** a client leaving interrupts its handler; a slow client holds back a streamed response; a body past its limit fails with `TooLarge`; a file is served streamed; SSE events arrive as sent; a request over TLS with a test certificate; `text` of invalid UTF-8 fails.
- [ ] **MB.10** `http: the client and the network capability`. `Nomi.Net` in nomi/core (D8), taking `Net` from `Nomi`: `onlyHosts`, `noListen` and `loopback`; `Nomi.Http.Client` on `fetch`. **Check:** a request to a host outside `onlyHosts` is refused, and so is a redirect to one; `listen` under `noListen` is refused; an interrupted request aborts its fetch; the loopback serves the same responses as a socket.
- [ ] **MB.11** `http: WebSockets`. `upgrade`, and a client. **Check:** messages both ways, on a socket and on the loopback; close codes; `send` waiting on backpressure until drain; the connection's fiber interrupted when the peer leaves.
- [ ] **MB.12** `core: crypto, random, passwords, compression`. Compression's stream transforms are tasks (D3). **Check:** hashes and HMACs against published vectors; random values seeded per test; argon2id hashes verify, and bcrypt ones; compression round-trips, streamed and whole, and corrupt input fails with `Invalid`.
- [ ] **MB.13** `http: routes, cookies and forms`. Pure Elm, signing cookies with MB.12's HMAC and streaming a multipart part through `Stream.reader`. **Check:** tables of paths, cookies (RFC 6265's examples, signed and tampered) and forms (urlencoded and multipart, a part streamed).
- [ ] **MB.14** `core: logging`. `Nomi.Log` with a `Logger` passed explicitly, as Elm passes dependencies, rather than annotations fibers carry (architecture §8 takes no FiberRefs); its own `Value`, so nomi/core needs no elm/json. **Check:** fields added with `with` are on every line its logger writes, and two loggers with different fields used by concurrent fibers never mix; text on a terminal, JSON otherwise; lines captured under `nomi test`.
- [ ] **MB.15** `xtask: services`. Postgres, MySQL, Valkey and Garage in containers, pinned by digest in references.toml (D3), which `cargo xtask services <name>` starts with podman or docker, waits for, runs its suite against and stops. A tier-2 suite: steps that touch it and every gate run it, and a Linux CI job runs it on push, as GitHub's macOS and Windows runners have no containers. Garage stands in for MinIO, which stopped publishing images in October 2025 and has been archived since ([Minimus](https://www.minimus.io/post/minio-docker-image-changes-how-to-find-a-secure-minio-alternative)). **Check:** each service starts from its pin and answers a smoke test; a missing container runtime fails loudly; nothing is left running; Garage presigns URLs and aborts a multipart upload.
- [ ] **MB.16** `sql: SQLite`. `Nomi.Sql` over `Bun.sql` (D9), on SQLite: `open`, `memory`, `query`, `execute`, `transaction`, row decoders, `int64` and `int64Value`. A SQLite statement runs on the event loop, so every fiber waits while it runs and an interrupted one ends once it returns, which its docs say. **Check:** queries and decoders on a file and in memory; a transaction rolls back on a database error, on the program's own error and on interruption; an integer past 2^53 round-trips exactly through `int64Value` and `int64` and fails `int` rather than rounding; `open` refuses a path outside its capability's root, opens read-only under `Fs.readOnly`, and fails with `NotOnDisk` on `Fs.memory`.
- [ ] **MB.17** `sql: Postgres and MySQL`. `connect` and its pool; a query whose fiber is interrupted cancelled from a connection kept outside the pool (`pg_cancel_backend`, `KILL QUERY`); `listen`. **Check:** MB.16's tests against both, `int64Value` into a `bigint` column included, under `cargo xtask services`; a query interrupted mid-run is cancelled on the server, with every pooled connection busy too; LISTEN delivers, and a lost connection fails its listener.
- [ ] **MB.18** `net: TCP, TLS, UDP and DNS`. **Check:** echo over TCP, TLS with a test certificate, and Unix sockets where the OS has them, on a socket and on `Net.loopback`; a connection upgraded to TLS; datagrams; lookups of `localhost`; `onlyHosts` holding for each; an interrupted fiber's socket closed.
- [ ] **MB.19** `redis`. Blocking commands and subscriptions get a connection of their own, so closing it on interruption fails no other fiber's commands. **Check:** the typed commands and `command` against Valkey; pub/sub; an interrupted blocking command closes its connection while another fiber's command on the same client succeeds; `Redis.memory` agrees with Valkey on the common commands.
- [ ] **MB.20** `s3`. **Check:** against Garage: put and get streamed, head, list, delete, presigned URLs; an interrupted upload is aborted; `S3.fromFs` agrees.
- [ ] **MB.21** `examples and review`. todo-server on SQLite, so its tests need no container; fullstack with a WebSocket; a CLI example; the README's server section; an exit review of MB as M12's, its fixes as steps. **Check:** the examples' tests; the review's must-fix findings fixed.
- [ ] **MB.22** `release: 0.7`.

**Exit:** server programs and every test suite run on Bun with no Node; each `nomi/*` module's fake and interruption guarantees are tested, on each OS CI runs where the OS has the feature; the services suite passes against Postgres, MySQL, Valkey and Garage; the examples use the platform and nothing else; and the tests, javascript and lsp corpus suites are unchanged.

## MP: Raw performance, release 0.8

Goal: nomi does the least work its job allows. M8b beat elm and elm-format on every cell; MP aims to beat them by multiples, and to make what has no upstream (the editor, tests, watch, the server runtime, workspaces) as lean: fewer cycles and instructions, fewer allocations and bytes copied, less peak and retained memory, better algorithms, shorter critical paths, and nothing busy while idle. Every trick is in scope (allocators, arenas, data layout, SIMD, LTO, PGO, better algorithms) as long as the code stays safe Rust, output stays byte-identical, a profile picked the change and a measurement kept it.

MP follows MB and covers all of MQ's, M11's, M12's and MB's code; measuring what exists can start once MQ ends. P.1–P.4 build the measurements; P.5 onward are areas, each refined into small steps, one root cause a commit, once P.4's cost map ranks it. Experiments that lose are recorded too.

**Where it starts.** [benchmarks.md](benchmarks.md) records M8b's 204 of 204 cells (exosphere cold under 0.19.3: 1.47 s wall, 5.29 s CPU, 702 MiB; warm 136 ms; docspell cold 1.11 s; elm-material-icons `--docs` 468 ms), and 9.22b the language server's (exosphere's first diagnostics 2.65 s, saves 27 ms, completion 30 ms, 890 MiB). MQ has moved all of them, and nothing yet measures allocations, live or retained heap, idle cost, hardware counters or latency tails. What is already in place is measured, not proposed again: mimalloc, the one-word interned `Text`, FxHash, HIR arenas, the chunked union-find, shared alias bodies, lazy interfaces, artifact reuse and DAG scheduling. `NOMI_TIMINGS` sums `Instant` durations across threads: it locates work but isn't CPU time.

**Measurement contract**, which `cargo xtask bench` enforces rather than this prose:
- Every result carries its provenance: revision and dirty state, binary hash, corpus pins, command and environment, machine, toolchain, build flags, allocator, threads, artifact-cache and page-cache state. Storage-sensitive changes are measured on a real disk, not `/tmp`'s RAM.
- Baseline and candidate are built first and run alternately under the same conditions, with nothing else running. Raw samples are kept; medians, spread and paired changes reported. An inconclusive comparison is repeated, never called a win.
- Two tiers: a fixed quick set, minutes long, for every experiment; the full sweep, its duration stated, to land one.
- Profiling is apart from timing: profiles from symbolized optimized builds, gains confirmed on the shipping binary and allocator without the profiler.
- What is measured: wall and user/system CPU; cycles, instructions, cache and branch misses and page faults where the hardware gives them; allocations, bytes allocated, peak live and retained heap, with an allocation profiler checked to see mimalloc's; whole-tree CPU and simultaneous memory for process trees; latency distributions, with enough samples for the percentile claimed, for the editor and server.
- Work as well as time: parses, inferences, decodes, bytes read and written, requests completed. Output is checked: a failed request, a stale diagnostic, a skipped test or a smaller output is never a speedup.
- Results go in `docs/benchmarks.md`, samples and profiles under the development cache by revision; no service or dashboard.

**Workloads**, from the pinned corpus: every buildable app (exosphere, docspell with its generated API, liascript, elm-3d-scene's shaders, concourse's entries and the rest) and the six documentation packages, plus the package corpus's measured extremes (large unions, records, aliases, matches, dependency graphs); `check`, development, `--debug`, `--optimize` and `--docs` under each Compat; builds with empty artifacts, with dependencies built, unchanged in a fresh process and in a live one, and after each kind of edit (a body, an interface, a widely imported module, the manifest, a failure and then its fix); formatting whole trees and largest modules, formatted or not, Markdown and shaders included; error workloads from mutated real modules; editor sessions from todomvc to concourse, captured and generated, past 1,000 edits for retention; the seven runnable test suites by worker count and reporter; M11's server and M12's workspace under recorded traffic; and xtask's own corpus runs.

- [ ] **P.1** `bench: the workload matrix`. The corpus inventoried by size, module counts and features; the quick set chosen; deterministic edits and sessions for the states above; missing archives and expected failures listed with their reasons, never counted as throughput. **Check:** each replay has a stable input digest, the work and output it expects, and a command that runs it alone in its own sandbox.
- [ ] **P.2** `bench: samples and the whole operation`. `Cost`/`best_of` keep samples and summarize comparable runs. The language server's benchmark fails on an error response, saves update text and disk, diagnostics are matched to their revision, and deadlines cover the whole exchange; it reports launch to first diagnostics as well as open to diagnostics. CPU ticks come from the platform, children's CPU is counted once, and idle CPU and memory are measured. **Check:** a failing or stale answer can't win, and the harness's own timing floor is reported.
- [ ] **P.3** `bench: profiles`. CPU (perf or samply), allocations (a profiler validated against mimalloc) and Bun's CPU, heap and GC profiles for the runtime and test runner, with entry points per phase only where the real workloads need them. **Check:** profiles resolve to nomi's functions, and unprofiled repetitions bound the profiler's overhead.
- [ ] **P.4** `bench: baseline and cost map`. The baseline frozen and published beside the old scoreboard; each user-facing operation's cost mapped to crate, function and critical path (inclusive and exclusive CPU, allocations, live and retained memory, repeated work), with whole-process profiles showing what is negligible; P.5 onward ranked by measured share, each with a numeric target set against the noise. **Check:** every ranking has a reproducible profile behind it.
- [ ] **P.5** `perf: release builds`. Thin and full LTO and codegen units first, since they change every later profile and the inlining that Q.6 had to mark by hand; then PGO, trained on P.1's workloads and validated on held-out projects and sessions; then allocator options against the whole matrix (B.3: huge pages won cold wall time and lost warm CPU and memory). `target-cpu=native` only as a labelled experiment. **Check:** the distributable binary improves beyond the noise, with binary size and build time recorded.
- [ ] **P.6** `perf: text, names and reports`. base and project: UTF-16 and Elm-column prefix scans, suggestions' vectors, document fitting, name hashing and the interner's lock. `Text` leaks each new spelling for the process's life, which a long editor session grows: bounded or scoped interning weighed against that retention.
- [ ] **P.7** `perf: syntax`. Lexing, lookahead, events, tree construction, validation, GLSL and the two error recognizers (SYN-03's borrowed names if their allocations show). A batch tree only if rowan dominates and the conversion pays; one grammar and lossless recovery stay.
- [ ] **P.8** `perf: lowering and canonicalization`. Copied environments and metadata, export lookups, data built for no consumer, shared union descriptions or compact ids.
- [ ] **P.9** `perf: type inference`. Constraints, copying and restoring, generalization, unification, record maps and the union-find; descriptor size and allocations per instantiation; the occurs check's active `Vec` and its revisits of shared subgraphs (CB02), with chain and DAG scaling checks.
- [ ] **P.10** `perf: patterns and optimization`. Exhaustiveness matrices, decision trees and branch sharing on morphir-elm and the corpus's extremes, with `.elmo` and `o.dat` bytes identical.
- [ ] **P.11** `perf: JavaScript generation`. One output buffer sized ahead, borrowed generation, one reachability visit per global, bytes emitted and copied measured. Elm's JS value representation is a compatibility decision, not a printer one.
- [ ] **P.12** `perf: formatting and Markdown`. Borrowed source, fewer strings per atom, `@docs` grouping by lookup (FMT-03), rendering straight into a sized buffer, Cheapskate's label parsing measured on real input; the dialect stays Cheapskate's.
- [ ] **P.13** `perf: engine, artifacts and scheduling`. VFS reads and canonicalization, the crawl, package memoization, artifact hashing and codecs, what each graph keeps alive and how `trash` frees it; hashing nomi's own 22 MB executable on every run to key the cache (CR.6), which may be a sixth of a warm build; thread counts with critical-path and lock-wait evidence, since busier cores are not less work.
- [ ] **P.14** `perf: registry and package commands`. Solves replayed against the pinned index, backtracking and `oldest` included; local CPU apart from transfer, through the fixture server; no network on warm local commands, and never a real publish.
- [ ] **P.15** `perf: language server`. Every provider and the protocol conversion, references across large generated modules among them (octicons-elm's take minutes unoptimized); first-use inference apart from later requests; requests during diagnostics, cancellations and several projects, for the index lock and queueing; 1,000 edits and more, and repeated opening and closing, for retention.
- [ ] **P.16** `perf: test runner and watch`. Discovery, compilation, loading, Bun's startup, execution, messages and reporters, by worker count. The watcher walks the tree every 100 ms: an event-driven one if it measures better and gives the same events.
- [ ] **P.17** `perf: fiber runtime and the server platform`. Bun's CPU, heap and GC profiles under real tasks and the server's traffic: continuations, run-queue compaction, scopes, joins, interruption, host calls and timers (`TestClock`'s sorted array); requests per second, CPU per request, p50/p95/p99, event-loop delay, and what is still alive once traffic drains. Fairness, the virtual clock's order, cancellation and graceful shutdown stay. Candidates from Effect 4.0.5's fiber runtime, read on 2026-10-15, when on Node 100,000 suspended fibers cost 1,240 bytes each here and 834 there, and a million chained steps took about 118 ms here and 83 there: an exit that is itself the task, as Effect's and elm/core's are, rather than a task wrapping an exit (two objects per `succeed`); a success handed straight to the next continuation, bounded at a fixed inline depth, rather than unwound through the loop; a fiber's observers, reapers and scope allocated when first used; one class per task kind, so property reads stay monomorphic; and a finished fiber's stack swapped for a shared empty one. Not candidates: its priority buckets, context caches, tracer hooks and immediate forks, which would change Elm's scheduling order.
- [ ] **P.18** `perf: workspaces and tooling`. Modules M12's members share built once; xtask and testkit on the full corpus, whose graphs run peaks at 8.6 GB.
- [ ] **P.19** `perf: SIMD and data layout`. With P.6–P.18's profiles: safe vectorized scanning (`memchr`/`memmem`, auto-vectorization checked in the assembly) in the lexer, `LineIndex`, literal, comment and GLSL scanning, JSON escaping and hashing; arenas, recycled buffers, compact ids, inline small collections and sorted vectors where measured sizes call for them. Stable Rust and the minimum version, no intrinsics and no `unsafe`, portable to CPUs without vector units; an architecture-specific gain counts only on hardware it was measured on.
- [ ] **P.20** `bench: final numbers`. The full matrix against the frozen baseline and the pinned upstream tools: before and after, spread, allocations and retention, attribution, replay commands, and what was tried and rejected. Deterministic work-count and scaling checks guard each kind of regression found; wall-clock budgets stay out of shared CI.
- [ ] **P.21** `release: 0.8`.

**Exit:** each user-facing operation has a measured cost map, failures, incrementality, long sessions and idle time included; every landed change has its before and after against the same baseline; every dominant cost has been investigated and what remains is accounted for; and output, diagnostics, every ratchet and corpus suite, and the runtime's properties are unchanged.

## Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Error-message parity is the longest pole: `Syntax.hs` is 5,904 lines, `Type.hs` 1,619 and `Canonicalize.hs` 1,258. | Titles and regions gate first, and message text ratchets to 100% by 0.2. |
| Formatter parity stalls on edge cases. | The formatter tree mirrors elm-format's AST, and rule 6 applies. |
| Upstream ships changes, such as 0.19.4 or server modules through Acadia. | One Compat per release; server code lives only in packages and kernels. |
| Scale: upstream is about 95k lines across the three tools. | Releases 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3 each replace one tool on their own. |
| Sessions don't share memory. | The plan, the tests and the history carry all state. |
| Some oracles need GHC. | Only tier 3 uses them; their snapshots are checked in. |
